# MSP430F5529 Debugging over Spy-Bi-Wire, Phase B1 (read-only) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Debug the firmware running on the user's MSP430F5529 board with `msp430-elf-gdb` (halt, resume, single-step, registers, memory, EEM hardware breakpoints) without any possibility of changing its flash.

**Architecture:** A new probe-side layer `msp430::Debugger` ports the CPUXv2 debug macros of TI's MSP Debug Stack onto the existing `Tap`; `msp430::Session` exposes it as operations 0x09–0x12 of vendor command 0x90, with new states, a write whitelist (`PROTECTED`) and a lock on Phase A's flash operations. On the host, `gdbserver.py` speaks GDB's remote protocol and drives the probe through new `probe.py` client methods; `symelf.py` turns name tables into a symbol-only ELF so GDB knows the MSP430X architecture and the firmware's symbols.

**Tech Stack:** C++20 (no heap, exceptions or RTTI) in `lib/msp430`, Zephyr 4.4 glue, Python 3 standard library for the host tool and its tests, TI MSP Debug Stack 3.15.1.1 (BSD-3-Clause files only) as the reference, `msp430-elf-gdb` from TI's MSP430-GCC.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-msp430-debug-design.md`. Phase B2 (full mode, software breakpoints, `load`) is not part of this plan.

---

## Read first

**Branch.** All work happens on `msp430-debug` (already checked out; the spec is committed there).

**Commits.** Plain commit messages in the style of the existing history. **Do not add a `Co-Authored-By` trailer or any other Claude/Anthropic attribution.** The user asked for this explicitly. Do not push.

**This board's firmware exists nowhere else.** Until Task 25 nothing in this plan touches the probe or the MSP430 board. Task 25 has its own safety rules; read them before you start it, and do not run any on-target command from an earlier task.

**Reference code.** The CPUXv2 sequences come from TI's MSP Debug Stack (MSPDS), open-source package 3.15.1.1, the current release. Download it once, outside the repository:

```bash
mkdir -p ~/Developer/_ref/mspds && cd ~/Developer/_ref/mspds
curl -sL -A "Mozilla/5.0" -o MSPDebugStack_OS_Package_3_15_1_1.zip \
  https://software-dl.ti.com/msp430/msp430_public_sw/mcu/msp430/MSPDS/3_15_1_001/export/MSPDebugStack_OS_Package_3_15_1_1.zip
shasum -a 256 MSPDebugStack_OS_Package_3_15_1_1.zip
# e3a59a98c43de7a92e5814d8c3304026165e6d2551e60acaca1f08c6b1a4bac8
unzip -q -o MSPDebugStack_OS_Package_3_15_1_1.zip -d pkg
head -3 pkg/revisions.txt        # Rev 3.15.1.001
ls pkg/Bios/src/hal/macros/ | grep -c Xv2
```

The files have CRLF line endings; read them with `tr -d '\r' < file`. The GitHub copy `osresearch/MSPDebugStack` (commit `f024f3433944ab497a02c1566031e86ecb93d7d0`, "import of MSPDebugStack 3.15.1.1") has identical `Bios/` sources apart from the line endings. Line numbers in this plan refer to these files.

The files this plan ports, all under `pkg/Bios/src/`:

| File | Used for |
|---|---|
| `hal/macros/SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2.c` | attach without reset, halt, re-sync after a stop |
| `hal/macros/SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2.c` | reset and halt |
| `hal/macros/RestoreContext_ReleaseJtagXv2.c` | resume, detach |
| `hal/macros/SingleStepXv2.c` | single step |
| `hal/macros/ReadAllCpuRegsXv2.c`, `WriteAllCpuRegsXv2.c` | registers |
| `hal/macros/ReadMemWordsXv2.c`, `WriteMemWordsXv2.c` | memory while halted |
| `hal/macros/EemDataExchangeXv2.c` | EEM register access, breakpoints |
| `hal/macros/WaitForEem.c` | stop detection |
| `hil/edt.h` (lines 369–432, 541–816), `hil/edt.c` (lines 44–57) | the helpers the macros inline: ReadMemWordXv2, WriteMemWordXv2, ReadCpuRegXv2, WriteCpuRegXv2, SetPcXv2, SyncJtagXv2, wait_for_synch, i_ReadJmbOut |
| `hal/JTAG_defs.h`, `hal/EEM_defs.h` | IR codes, control-signal bits, EEM registers |
| `hil/fet_generic/hil_2w.c` (lines 145–210, 336–352) | 2-wire shift framing, 32-bit shift |

**Licence check (done while writing this plan).** Every file above carries TI's BSD-3-Clause notice ("Copyright (C) 2012 Texas Instruments Incorporated", the three redistribution conditions and the disclaimer); the same holds for the 3.3.1.3 device-database files used below for the F5529's parameters. The package also contains TSPA-licensed code (`DLL430_v3/src/TI/DLL430/EnergyTrace_TSPA`); nothing is taken from it. `PollJStateReg.c` is BSD-3-Clause but is not ported: the F5529 polls breakpoints with `WaitForEem` (DLL `PollingManager.cpp`, `mPollingMacro[PT_BREAKPOINT] = ID_WaitForEem`), and JSTATE polling needs 64-bit shifts and serves LPMx.5 and EnergyTrace only.

**Device parameters of the F5529.** The 3.15.1.1 package does not contain TI's device database (the DLL loads it from a separate XML archive). The last open-source release that has it in source form, 3.3.1.3 (`TemplateDeviceDb`, GitHub `robertinant/MSPDebugStack_OS_Package`, commit `9fe38c15e6d3cdaf45c1fc2792726eed5b9d2cf7`), gives for the F5529 (`MSP430F55xx.cpp`, `MSP430F5xxx.h`, `MSP430Defaults.h`, `Registration.h`):

- `LargeEemMode` → `LargeTrigger`: **8 memory-bus triggers**, 2 register-write triggers, 10 combinations. These are the breakpoints (`kHwBreakpoints = 8`). The datasheet calls this EEM version "L".
- `ExtendedFeatures<..., _1377 = true, ...>` → `altRomAddressForCpuRead = 1` (also the firmware default in `fw/uifv1/HalGlobalVars430.c:90`): `ReadCpuRegXv2` moves a register to address 0x0FF6, not to the mailbox.
- no `PowerSettings` → `DisableLpmx5()` does nothing on this device.
- clock control `GCC_EXTENDED`; module clock control default 0x041F (not ported, see below).

```bash
for f in MSP430F55xx.cpp MSP430F5xxx.h MSP430Defaults.h Registration.h; do
  gh api "repos/robertinant/MSPDebugStack_OS_Package/contents/DLL430_v3/src/TI/DLL430/TemplateDeviceDb/$f?ref=9fe38c15e6d3cdaf45c1fc2792726eed5b9d2cf7" \
    --jq .content | base64 -d > ~/Developer/_ref/mspds/$f
done
grep -n "LargeTrigger\|LargeEemMode" ~/Developer/_ref/mspds/MSP430Defaults.h
```

**GDB's register layout (decided while writing this plan).** `gdb/msp430-tdep.c` in binutils-gdb (sourceware master, commit `5a1c88b169767f282bd232d066b37a87df310555`, unchanged in this respect since 2012) defines 16 raw registers in the order R0 (PC), R1 (SP), R2 (SR), R3 (CG), R4–R15, every raw register of type `uint32` (4 bytes) for both MSP430 and MSP430X (`msp430_register_type` / `msp430x_register_type`, lines 159–184), and `pc`, `sp`, `sr`, `cg`, `r4`… as pseudo-registers on top. The gdbarch never calls `tdesc_use_registers`, so a target description cannot change this layout. **The server therefore sends no target description**: a `g` reply is 16 × 4 bytes, little-endian, 20-bit values zero-extended, and `p`/`P` use raw numbers 0–15. Task 22 checks this against the installed `msp430-elf-gdb` (`maint print remote-registers`) before anything runs on the board.

The same file chooses the instruction set and code model from the ELF file's `.MSP430.attributes` section (`msp430_gdbarch_init`): without it GDB assumes the 16-bit MSP430 with 16-bit addresses. `symelf.py` writes that section (ISA MSP430X, large code model); the tag numbers come from binutils `include/elf/msp430.h` (`OFBA_MSPABI_Tag_ISA = 4`, `Tag_Code_Model = 6`, `Tag_Data_Model = 8`, vendor `"mspabi"`, section type `SHT_MSP430_ATTRIBUTES = 0x70000003`).

**Conventions carried over from Phase A.**

- Host tests use `tests/host/testing.hpp` (`TEST`, `CHECK`, `CHECK_EQ`, `CHECK_BYTES`, `REQUIRE`). All test files compile into the single binary `dap_tests`.
- `FakeTap` logs every TAP call as a short string (`ir:28`, `dr16:0000`, `dr20:04524`, `dr32:00000088`, `tclk:0`, `tclk:1`, `delay:40`, `open`, `close`). The `Debugger` tests compare these transcripts with builders in `tests/host/debug_logs.hpp` that were written from the MSPDS sources, as Phase A did with SLAU320.
- IR constants are already bit-reversed, exactly as MSPDS's `JTAG_defs.h` writes them (`IR_CNTRL_SIG_CAPTURE` is `0x28` in both).
- Python tests use only the standard library and the `FakeFirmware` transport; nothing needs a probe.

**Hardware-verification boundary.** Tasks 1–21 need no hardware and no GDB. Task 22 asks the user before installing GDB; Task 23 needs it. Task 25 is the only task that touches the board.

## Debugging a CPUXv2 in one page

- **Taking a running CPU** (`SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2`): if the EEM has not stopped the CPU and the CPU is not in a low-power mode, trigger block 0 is set to "any instruction fetch" with a break reaction, the EEM is enabled and the CPU stops on its next fetch; trigger block 0 is then restored. JTAG then synchronizes (control signal 0x1501 until the sync bit 0x0200), clocks the CPU until its pipe is empty (CPUSUSP, 0x0100), captures the memory address bus (PC = MAB − 4, or − 2 in a low-power mode), holds the watchdog (keeping its other bits) and saves SR, clearing CPUOFF and GIE in the CPU so that JTAG-fed instructions execute. Nothing is reset.
- **Reset and halt** (`SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2`): sync, power-on reset through the control-signal register, PC from the reset vector at 0xFFFE.
- **Registers**: the CPU executes `MOVA Rn, &0x0FF6` fed through JTAG and the value is captured from the data bus; writes feed `MOVA #imm20, Rn`. PC and SR live in the saved context and are written back on release.
- **Memory while halted**: address and data registers per word (`ReadMemWordsXv2`, `WriteMemWordsXv2`); unlike Phase A's `ReadMemQuick`, these do not move the PC.
- **Release** (`RestoreContext_ReleaseJtagXv2`): SR, WDTCTL and PC back, one clock, EEM control (0x0007 = EEM on, breakpoints armed; 0x0006 = EEM off), `IR_CNTRL_SIG_RELEASE`.
- **Breakpoints**: EEM trigger block *i* (registers at 8·*i*: value, control, mask, combination) compares the MAB on instruction fetches; combination *i* in `BREAKREACT` (0x80) stops the CPU. Stop detection reads the EEM control register for `EEM_STOPPED` (0x80) (`WaitForEem`).
- **Single step**: trigger block 0 is borrowed for "next instruction fetch", the CPU is released, stops after one instruction, and is taken again.
- **Clocks while halted**: the sync writes the EEM general clock control 0x000E (MCLK, SMCLK, ACLK stopped while halted). The reset-and-halt path of MSPDS also programs per-module clock control from the DLL's device database; this port leaves that out, so the modules keep their reset defaults. Task 25 measures whether Timer0_B runs while halted.

### Task 2: 32-bit DR shifts (`Tap::dr_shift32`)

EEM registers are read and written with 32-bit data-register shifts (MSPDS `SetReg_32Bits`, `hil_2w.c:336-352`, same framing as the 16- and 20-bit shifts, no de-scramble). A 32-bit scan has 3 + 32 + 2 = 37 cycles, more than the 32 one `SbwPort::cycles()` call can clock, so `SbwTap::scan` splits long scans. Between two calls SBWTCK stays high (the port's contract) and the TAP stays in Shift-DR; SBW limits only the low phase of SBWTCK. The PIO driver already splits every call into 9-cycle words with the same pause, so nothing new happens on the wire.

**Files:**
- Modify: `tests/host/test_sbw_tap.cpp`
- Modify: `lib/msp430/include/msp430/tap.hpp`
- Modify: `lib/msp430/include/msp430/sbw_tap.hpp`
- Modify: `lib/msp430/src/sbw_tap.cpp`
- Modify: `tests/host/fake_tap.hpp`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Append to `tests/host/test_sbw_tap.cpp`:

```cpp
TEST(sbw_tap_dr32_is_split_into_two_port_calls)
{
	MockSbwPort p;
	SbwTap t{p};
	t.reset();
	p.cycle_calls.clear();
	t.dr_shift32(0x80000083);
	/* 3 lead-in + 32 data + 2 trailing cycles = 37: one call of 32, one of 5.
	 * cycle: 0 (1,T) 1 (0,1) 2 (0,1) | 3..34 data, TMS=1 on 34 | 35 (1,1) | 36 (0,T)
	 * 0x80000083 MSB first: TDI=1 on cycles 3, 27, 33, 34.
	 * first call (cycles 0..31): tms = bit 0          = 0x00000001
	 *                            tdi = bits 0,1,2,3,27 = 0x0800000F
	 * second call (cycles 32..36): tms = 34, 35 -> bits 2, 3 = 0x0C
	 *                              tdi = 33, 34, 35, 36 -> bits 1..4 = 0x1E */
	REQUIRE(p.cycle_calls.size() == 2u);
	CHECK_EQ(p.cycle_calls[0].count, 32u);
	CHECK_EQ(p.cycle_calls[0].tms, 0x00000001u);
	CHECK_EQ(p.cycle_calls[0].tdi, 0x0800000Fu);
	CHECK_EQ(p.cycle_calls[1].count, 5u);
	CHECK_EQ(p.cycle_calls[1].tms, 0x0Cu);
	CHECK_EQ(p.cycle_calls[1].tdi, 0x1Eu);
	REQUIRE(!p.scans.empty());
	CHECK(p.scans.back() == (MockSbwPort::Scan{false, 32, 0x80000083}));
	CHECK_EQ(p.state, MockSbwPort::RTI);
	CHECK(!p.tclk_glitch);
	CHECK(!p.bad_count);
}

TEST(sbw_tap_dr32_assembles_tdo_across_the_calls)
{
	MockSbwPort p;
	SbwTap t{p};
	t.reset();
	/* 0xC0000003: data bits on cycles 3, 4 (first call) and 33, 34 (second
	 * call, bits 1 and 2). */
	p.tdo_script.push_back((1u << 3) | (1u << 4));
	p.tdo_script.push_back((1u << 1) | (1u << 2));
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift32(0), 0xC0000003u);
}

TEST(sbw_tap_dr32_with_tclk_low_keeps_tclk_low)
{
	MockSbwPort p;
	SbwTap t{p};
	t.reset();
	t.clr_tclk();
	p.cycle_calls.clear();
	t.dr_shift32(0x12345678);
	REQUIRE(p.cycle_calls.size() == 2u);
	CHECK_EQ(p.cycle_calls[0].tdi & 1u, 0u);          /* cycle 0: TDI = TCLK = 0 */
	CHECK_EQ((p.cycle_calls[1].tdi >> 4) & 1u, 0u);   /* cycle 36: TDI = TCLK = 0 */
	CHECK(!p.tclk_glitch);
	CHECK(!p.tclk);
	REQUIRE(!p.scans.empty());
	CHECK_EQ(p.scans.back().value, 0x12345678u);
}
```

In `lib/msp430/src/sbw_tap.cpp`:

Replace:

```cpp
 * does not change while the TAP leaves and re-enters Run-Test/Idle.
 *
 * `nbits` must be 1..26 for an IR scan (4 lead-in cycles) and 1..27 for a DR
 * scan (3 lead-in cycles), because head + nbits + 2 cycles must fit the 32
 * cycles of one port call; callers pass 8, 16 and 20.
 */
uint32_t SbwTap::scan(bool ir, unsigned nbits, uint32_t data)
{
	const unsigned head = ir ? 4u : 3u;
	uint32_t tms = ir ? 0b0011u : 0b001u;
	uint32_t tdi = (ir ? 0b1110u : 0b110u) | (tclk_ ? 1u : 0u);

	for (unsigned k = 0; k < nbits; ++k) {
		if ((data >> (nbits - 1u - k)) & 1u) {
			tdi |= 1u << (head + k);
		}
	}
	tms |= 1u << (head + nbits - 1u); /* last data bit: Exit1 */
	tms |= 1u << (head + nbits);      /* Update */
	tdi |= 1u << (head + nbits);
	if (tclk_) {
		tdi |= 1u << (head + nbits + 1u); /* Run-Test/Idle: TDI = TCLK */
	}

	const uint32_t tdo = port_.cycles(tms, tdi, head + nbits + 2u);

	uint32_t out = 0;
	for (unsigned k = 0; k < nbits; ++k) {
		out = (out << 1) | ((tdo >> (head + k)) & 1u);
	}
	return out;
```

with:

```cpp
 * does not change while the TAP leaves and re-enters Run-Test/Idle.
 *
 * `nbits` is 1..32. One port call clocks at most 32 cycles, so a longer scan
 * (the 32-bit DR scan has 3 + 32 + 2 = 37 cycles) is split into several
 * calls. Between two calls SBWTCK stays high and the TAP stays in Shift-DR;
 * SBW limits only the low phase of SBWTCK, not the high phase.
 */
uint32_t SbwTap::scan(bool ir, unsigned nbits, uint32_t data)
{
	const unsigned head = ir ? 4u : 3u;
	const unsigned total = head + nbits + 2u;
	uint64_t tms = ir ? 0b0011u : 0b001u;
	uint64_t tdi = (ir ? 0b1110u : 0b110u) | (tclk_ ? 1u : 0u);

	for (unsigned k = 0; k < nbits; ++k) {
		if ((data >> (nbits - 1u - k)) & 1u) {
			tdi |= uint64_t{1} << (head + k);
		}
	}
	tms |= uint64_t{1} << (head + nbits - 1u); /* last data bit: Exit1 */
	tms |= uint64_t{1} << (head + nbits);      /* Update */
	tdi |= uint64_t{1} << (head + nbits);
	if (tclk_) {
		tdi |= uint64_t{1} << (head + nbits + 1u); /* Run-Test/Idle: TDI = TCLK */
	}

	uint64_t tdo = 0;
	for (unsigned done = 0; done < total;) {
		const unsigned n = (total - done > 32u) ? 32u : total - done;
		const uint32_t mask = (n == 32u) ? 0xFFFFFFFFu : ((1u << n) - 1u);
		const uint32_t part = port_.cycles(static_cast<uint32_t>(tms >> done) & mask,
						   static_cast<uint32_t>(tdi >> done) & mask, n);
		tdo |= static_cast<uint64_t>(part & mask) << done;
		done += n;
	}

	uint32_t out = 0;
	for (unsigned k = 0; k < nbits; ++k) {
		out = (out << 1) | static_cast<uint32_t>((tdo >> (head + k)) & 1u);
	}
	return out;
```

Then replace:

```cpp
}

/* SetTCLK_sbw / ClrTCLK_sbw: one Run-Test/Idle cycle whose TDI slot carries
 * the new TCLK level; the TMS slot uses TMSLDH when TCLK is currently high. */
```

with:

```cpp
}

 * de-scramble. */
uint32_t SbwTap::dr_shift32(uint32_t value)
{
	return scan(false, 32, value);
}

/* SetTCLK_sbw / ClrTCLK_sbw: one Run-Test/Idle cycle whose TDI slot carries
 * the new TCLK level; the TMS slot uses TMSLDH when TCLK is currently high. */
```

```cpp
		}

	uint32_t dr_shift32_(uint32_t value)
	{
		log.push_back(fmt("dr32:%08x", value));
		if (ir_ != msp430::kIrEmexDataExchange32) {
			return 0;
		}
		if (!eem_addr_pending_) {
			eem_addr_ = static_cast<uint8_t>(value);
			eem_addr_pending_ = true;
			return 0;
		}
		eem_addr_pending_ = false;
		const uint8_t reg = static_cast<uint8_t>(eem_addr_ & 0xFEu);
		if ((eem_addr_ & 1u) != 0) {
			return eem.count(reg) != 0 ? eem[reg] : 0;
		}
		eem[reg] = value;
		eem_writes.push_back({reg, value});
		return 0;
	}
```

### Task 3: `Cpuxv2::identify()`

Attaching without reset needs Phase A's entry, JTAG-ID, lock and core-ID steps, but not its sync and power-on reset. `connect()` is split: `identify()` is its first half, unchanged in behaviour, and `connect()` calls it. `in_full_emulation()` becomes public for the debug layer. /* GetDevice_430Xv2 = identify() + SyncJtag_AssertPor + read of the device ID. */
```cpp
Status Cpuxv2::connect(EntryMode mode, uint32_t slot_hz, Identity &id)
{
	const Status st = identify(mode, slot_hz, id);
	if (st != Status::Ok) {
		return st;
	}
	uint8_t raw[2] = {};
	if (!sync_por() || read_mem(id.device_id_ptr + 4u, 1, raw) != Status::Ok) {
		tap_.close();
		return Status::TargetError;
```

### Task 4: FakeTap debug model

The `Debugger` tests need the fake to answer what the MSPDS macros read: memory words after an address load (`ReadMemWordXv2`), CPU-register words (`ReadCpuRegXv2` captures two words without an address load), the memory address bus (`IR_ADDR_CAPTURE`), and the EEM control register. WDTCTL reads back with its read password 0x69, as on the chip, so a second sync after a first one sees a valid watchdog.

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/host/test_fake_tap.cpp`
- Modify: `tests/host/CMakeLists.txt`
- Modify: `tests/host/fake_tap.hpp`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/host/test_fake_tap.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "testing.hpp"
#include "fake_tap.hpp"

/* The debug-layer model of the test double behaves as the Debugger tests
 * assume. */

TEST(fake_tap_reads_memory_after_an_address_load)
{
	FakeTap t;
	t.memory[0x2400] = 0x1234;
	t.captured = {0xAAAA};
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrAddr16Bit);
	t.dr_shift20(0x2400);
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrDataCapture);
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift16(0), 0x1234);
	/* no address pending: the next capture is a CPU register word */
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift16(0), 0xAAAA);
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift16(0), 0x0000);
}

TEST(fake_tap_memory_writes_and_the_watchdog_password)
{
	FakeTap t;
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrAddr16Bit);
	t.dr_shift20(0x015C);
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrDataToAddr);
	t.dr_shift16(0x5A80);
	CHECK_EQ(t.memory[0x015C], 0x6980); /* WDTCTL reads back with 0x69 */
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrAddr16Bit);
	t.dr_shift20(0x2400);
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrDataToAddr);
	t.dr_shift16(0xBEEF);
	CHECK_EQ(t.memory[0x2400], 0xBEEF);
	REQUIRE(t.mem_writes.size() == 2u);
}

TEST(fake_tap_eem_exchange_and_control)
{
	FakeTap t;
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrEmexDataExchange32);
	t.dr_shift32(0x10);       /* write TB2 value */
	t.dr_shift32(0x4600);
	t.dr_shift32(0x11);       /* read it back */
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift32(0), 0x4600u);
	REQUIRE(t.eem_writes.size() == 1u);
	CHECK_EQ(t.eem_writes[0].first, 0x10);
	t.eem_control_script = {0x0080};
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrEmexReadControl);
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift16(0), 0x0080);
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift16(0), 0x0000);
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrEmexWriteControl);
	t.dr_shift16(0x0007);
	REQUIRE(t.eem_control_writes.size() == 1u);
	CHECK_EQ(t.eem_control_writes[0], 0x0007);
	t.mab_script = {0x14A2C};
	t.ir_shift(msp430::kIrAddrCapture);
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift20(0), 0x14A2Cu);
	CHECK_EQ(t.dr_shift20(0), 0x04524u);
}
```

```cpp
	/* Word memory read through IR_DATA_CAPTURE right after an address was
	 * loaded with IR_ADDR_16BIT (ReadMemWordXv2); writes through

	std::map<uint32_t, uint16_t> memory = {
		{0x015C, 0x6904}, {0x0144, 0x9658}, {0x1A04, 0x2955}, {0xFFFE, 0x441A}};
	/* DR16 under IR_DATA_CAPTURE when no address is pending: the two words
	 * (low, high) of every CPU register read (ReadCpuRegXv2). Default 0. */
	std::deque<uint16_t> captured;
	uint32_t mab = 0x04524;              /* DR20 under IR_ADDR_CAPTURE */
	std::deque<uint32_t> mab_script;
	uint16_t eem_control = 0x0000;       /* DR16 under IR_EMEX_READ_CONTROL */
	std::deque<uint16_t> eem_control_script;
```

### Task 5: `Debugger`: attach without reset, detach

This task creates the debug layer with its whole interface, the JTAG helpers it inlines from MSPDS `edt.h`, the conditional sync that takes a running CPU, the context restore, and `attach`/`detach`. Later tasks add one group of operations each. Read `SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2.c` (lines 70–329) and `RestoreContext_ReleaseJtagXv2.c` (lines 69–196) side by side with the code; every helper names its MSPDS source and lines, and the file header lists every place where the port deliberately differs.

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/host/debug_logs.hpp`
- Create: `tests/host/test_debugger.cpp`
- Modify: `tests/host/CMakeLists.txt`
- Create: `lib/msp430/include/msp430/debug.hpp`
- Create: `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`
- Modify: `lib/msp430/CMakeLists.txt`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the expected transcripts**

These builders transcribe the MSPDS helpers and macros into FakeTap log entries, one MSPDS statement per entry, with the source lines named above each. Write them from the MSPDS sources, not from `debug.cpp`. Create `tests/host/debug_logs.hpp`:

```cpp
#pragma once
/* Expected FakeTap transcripts of the MSPDS CPUXv2 macros, written from
 * TI's sources (MSPDS 3.15.1.1, Bios/src/hil/edt.h and
 * Bios/src/hal/macros/<macro>Xv2.c), for the JTAG ID 0x91 paths and the
 * MSP430F5529's device settings (altRomAddressForCpuRead = 1, no LPMx.5 test
 * registers). File and lines are named above each builder. */
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstdio>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

#include "testing.hpp"

namespace dbglog {

using Log = std::vector<std::string>;

inline Log operator+(Log a, const Log &b)
{
	a.insert(a.end(), b.begin(), b.end());
	return a;
}

inline std::string f(const char *fmt, unsigned v)
{
	char buf[32];
	std::snprintf(buf, sizeof(buf), fmt, v);
	return buf;
}
inline std::string ir(unsigned v) { return f("ir:%02x", v); }
inline std::string d16(unsigned v) { return f("dr16:%04x", v); }
inline std::string d20(unsigned v) { return f("dr20:%05x", v); }
inline std::string d32(unsigned v) { return f("dr32:%08x", v); }
inline const Log kTclk = {"tclk:0", "tclk:1"}; /* IHIL_TCLK() */

inline void check_log(const char *file, int line, const Log &got, const Log &want)
{
	if (got == want) {
		return;
	}
	size_t i = 0;
	while (i < got.size() && i < want.size() && got[i] == want[i]) {
		++i;
	}
	std::string msg = "TAP transcript differs at entry " + std::to_string(i) + "\n  want:";
	for (size_t k = i; k < want.size() && k < i + 12; ++k) {
		msg += " " + want[k];
	}
	msg += "\n  got: ";
	for (size_t k = i; k < got.size() && k < i + 12; ++k) {
		msg += " " + got[k];
	}
	::testing::fail(file, line, msg);
}
/* Variadic, so that a braced list with commas needs no extra parentheses. */
#define CHECK_LOG(got, ...) dbglog::check_log(__FILE__, __LINE__, got, (__VA_ARGS__))

inline bool contains(const Log &log, const Log &sub)
{
	for (size_t i = 0; i + sub.size() <= log.size(); ++i) {
		if (std::equal(sub.begin(), sub.end(), log.begin() + static_cast<long>(i))) {
			return true;
		}
	}
	return false;
}

/* Everything after the first occurrence of `marker`. */
inline Log after(const Log &log, const Log &marker)
{
	for (size_t i = 0; i + marker.size() <= log.size(); ++i) {
		if (std::equal(marker.begin(), marker.end(), log.begin() + static_cast<long>(i))) {
			return Log(log.begin() + static_cast<long>(i + marker.size()), log.end());
		}
	}
	return {};
}

/* Cpuxv2::identify: GetCoreID, IsLockKeyProgrammed, GetCoreipIdXv2 */
inline Log identify_log()
{
	return {"speed:250000", "open", "enter:high", "ir:28",
		"ir:28", "dr16:aaaa", "ir:28", "dr16:aaaa", "ir:28", "dr16:aaaa",
		"ir:e8", "dr16:0000", "ir:e1", "dr20:00000"};
}

/* ReadMemWordXv2, edt.h:369-383 */
inline Log read_word(unsigned addr)
{
	return {"tclk:0", ir(0xC1), d20(addr), "tclk:1", "tclk:0", ir(0x42), d16(0),
		"tclk:1", "tclk:0", "tclk:1"};
}

/* WriteMemWordXv2, edt.h:416-432 */
inline Log write_word(unsigned addr, unsigned data)
{
	return {"tclk:0", ir(0xC8), d16(0x0500), ir(0xC1), d20(addr), "tclk:1",
		ir(0xA1), d16(data), "tclk:0", ir(0xC8), d16(0x0501), "tclk:1",
		"tclk:0", "tclk:1"};
}

/* ReadCpuRegXv2, edt.h:541-589, altRomAddressForCpuRead = 1 */
inline Log read_reg(unsigned mova)
{
	return Log{ir(0x28), "tclk:0", ir(0x82), "tclk:1", d16(mova), ir(0xC8), d16(0x1401),
		   ir(0x82)} + kTclk + Log{d16(0x0FF6)} + kTclk +
	       Log{d16(0x3FFD), "tclk:0", ir(0xC8), d16(0x0501), ir(0x42), "tclk:1", d16(0)} +
	       kTclk + Log{d16(0)} + kTclk + kTclk + kTclk + Log{"tclk:0", ir(0x42), "tclk:1"};
}

/* WriteCpuRegXv2, edt.h:651-675 */
inline Log write_reg(unsigned mova, unsigned data)
{
	return Log{"tclk:0", ir(0x82), "tclk:1", d16(mova), ir(0xC8), d16(0x1401), ir(0x82)} +
	       kTclk + Log{d16(data)} + kTclk + Log{d16(0x3FFD)} + kTclk +
	       Log{"tclk:0", ir(0x21), d20(0), "tclk:1", ir(0xC8), d16(0x0501)} + kTclk +
	       Log{"tclk:0", ir(0x42), "tclk:1"};
}

/* SetPcXv2, edt.h:728-749 */
inline Log set_pc(unsigned pc)
{
	const unsigned mova = 0x0080u | ((pc >> 8) & 0x0F00u);
	return {ir(0x28), d16(0), "tclk:0", ir(0x82), "tclk:1", d16(mova), "tclk:0",
		ir(0xC8), d16(0x1400), ir(0x82), "tclk:0", "tclk:1", d16(pc & 0xFFFFu),
		"tclk:0", "tclk:1", d16(0x4303), "tclk:0", ir(0x21), d20(0)};
}

/* EemDataExchangeXv2: one read / one write (IR selected by the caller) */
inline Log eem_read(unsigned reg) { return {d32(reg | 1u), d32(0)}; }
inline Log eem_write(unsigned reg, unsigned value) { return {d32(reg), d32(value)}; }

/* The EEM part of SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2.c:95-168 when the CPU
 * is running and not in a low-power mode: trigger block 0 is saved, set to
 * "next instruction fetch", the EEM stops the CPU (`polls` control reads
 * until EEM_STOPPED), trigger block 0 is restored with `tb0` (VAL, CTL, MSK,
 * CMB, BREAKREACT). */
inline Log eem_stop(unsigned polls, const unsigned tb0[5])
{
	Log l = {ir(0xD0), d16(0), ir(0x28), d16(0), ir(0xB0)};
	l = l + eem_read(0x00) + eem_read(0x02) + eem_read(0x04) + eem_read(0x06) +
	    eem_read(0x80) + eem_write(0x02, 0) + eem_write(0x04, 0xFFFFF) +
	    eem_write(0x06, 1) + eem_write(0x80, 1) + eem_write(0x88, 0x000E) +
	    Log{ir(0x30), d16(0x0007)};
	for (unsigned i = 0; i < polls; ++i) {
		l = l + Log{ir(0xD0), d16(0)};
	}
	return l + Log{ir(0xB0)} + eem_write(0x00, tb0[0]) + eem_write(0x02, tb0[1]) +
	       eem_write(0x04, tb0[2]) + eem_write(0x06, tb0[3]) + eem_write(0x80, tb0[4]);
}

/* SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2.c:172-329 from "enable clock control
 * before sync" on, with sync, wait bit and CPUSUSP present at the first
 * look, followed by the FCTL3 check, the watchdog hold and the SR save.
 * `wdt` is the WDTCTL value read, `sr` the SR value read. */
inline Log sync_tail(unsigned wdt, unsigned sr)
{
	return Log{ir(0x30), d16(0x0005),
		   /* SyncJtagXv2 */
		   ir(0xC8), d16(0x1501), ir(0x28), d16(0),
		   ir(0x30), d16(0x0007), d16(0x0006),
		   ir(0xC8), d16(0x1501), ir(0x28), d16(0),
		   /* pipe empty on the first clock */
		   ir(0x28), "tclk:0", d16(0), "tclk:1",
		   ir(0xC8), d16(0x0501)} +
	       kTclk + Log{ir(0x21), d20(0), ir(0x28), d16(0), ir(0x30), d16(0x000E),
			   ir(0x28), d16(0)} +
	       read_word(0x0144) + read_word(0x015C) +
	       write_word(0x015C, 0x5A80u | (wdt & 0xFFu)) + read_reg(0x0260) +
	       write_reg(0x0082, sr & 0xFFE7u);
}

/* Clearing all 8 trigger blocks: CMB = 0 each, BREAKREACT = 0 */
inline Log clear_triggers()
{
	Log l = {ir(0xB0)};
	for (unsigned i = 0; i < 8; ++i) {
		l = l + eem_write(i * 8 + 6, 0);
	}
	return l + eem_write(0x80, 0);
}

/* RestoreContext_ReleaseJtagXv2.c:69-196, mdb = 0, ulpDebug = 0, CPU not
 * in the HALT state at the release clock. */
inline Log restore_release(unsigned pc, unsigned sr, unsigned wdt, unsigned eem_control)
{
	return write_reg(0x0082, sr) + write_word(0x015C, 0x5A00u | (wdt & 0xFFu)) +
	       set_pc((sr & 0x0010u) ? pc - 2 : pc) +
	       Log{"tclk:1", ir(0xC8), d16(0x0401), ir(0x21), ir(0x28), "tclk:0", d16(0),
		   "tclk:1", ir(0x30), d16(eem_control), ir(0x28), ir(0xA8)};
}

} // namespace dbglog
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/host/test_debugger.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "testing.hpp"
#include "fake_tap.hpp"
#include "debug_logs.hpp"
#include "msp430/debug.hpp"

#include <algorithm>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

using msp430::AttachMode;
using msp430::Debugger;
using msp430::DetachAction;
using msp430::Status;
using msp430::StopReason;
using namespace dbglog;

namespace {

struct Fx {
	FakeTap t;
	msp430::Cpuxv2 cpu{t};
	Debugger d{t, cpu};
	msp430::Identity id;

	/* The CPU runs and is not in a low-power mode: the first EEM control
	 * read says "not stopped", the one after the stop request "stopped".
	 * The SR read during the sync returns `sr`. */
	void running_cpu(uint16_t sr = 0x0008)
	{
		t.eem_control_script = {0x0000, 0x0080};
		t.captured = {sr, 0x0000};
	}

	Status attach()
	{
		running_cpu();
		return d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, id);
	}

	/* attach(), then forget the transcript and the recordings */
	void attached()
	{
		REQUIRE(attach() == Status::Ok);
		t.log.clear();
		t.mem_writes.clear();
		t.eem_writes.clear();
		t.eem_control_writes.clear();
	}
};

const unsigned kTb0Zero[5] = {0, 0, 0, 0, 0};

} // namespace

TEST(debugger_attach_running_follows_mspds_conditional_sync)
{
	Fx f;
	CHECK(f.attach() == Status::Ok);
	const Log want = identify_log() + eem_stop(1, kTb0Zero) + sync_tail(0x6904, 0x0008) +
			 read_word(0x1A04) + clear_triggers() + Log{"ir:28", "dr16:0000"};
	CHECK_LOG(f.t.log, want);
	CHECK(f.d.attached());
	CHECK(!f.d.running());
	CHECK(f.d.stop_reason() == StopReason::Request);
	CHECK_EQ(f.id.device_id, 0x2955);
}

TEST(debugger_attach_saves_pc_sr_and_watchdog)
{
	Fx f;
	f.t.mab = 0x14A2C; /* the MAB points 4 bytes past the next instruction */
	f.t.memory[0x015C] = 0x6936;
	f.running_cpu(0x0009); /* GIE + C */
	REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK_EQ(f.d.pc(), 0x14A28u);
	/* watchdog held, its other bits kept; SR written back without GIE */
	REQUIRE(f.t.mem_writes.size() == 1u); /* SR goes through MOVA, not a memory write */
	CHECK_EQ(f.t.mem_writes[0].first, 0x015Cu);
	CHECK_EQ(f.t.mem_writes[0].second, 0x5AB6);
	CHECK(contains(f.t.log, write_reg(0x0082, 0x0001)));
}

TEST(debugger_attach_restores_trigger_block_0)
{
	Fx f;
	f.t.eem[0x00] = 0x04400;
	f.t.eem[0x02] = 0x0000;
	f.t.eem[0x04] = 0x00000;
	f.t.eem[0x06] = 0x0001;
	f.t.eem[0x80] = 0x0001;
	REQUIRE(f.attach() == Status::Ok);
	const unsigned tb0[5] = {0x04400, 0, 0, 1, 1};
	CHECK(contains(f.t.log, eem_stop(1, tb0)));
}

TEST(debugger_attach_in_each_low_power_mode)
{
	/* LPM0..LPM4: CPUOFF plus SCG0, SCG1, OSCOFF as the mode requires, GIE set */
	for (uint16_t sr : {0x0018, 0x0058, 0x0098, 0x00D8, 0x00F8}) {
		Fx f;
		f.t.cntrl_sig = 0x0321; /* CPUOFF: the CPU is off */
		f.t.eem_control_script = {0x0000};
		f.t.captured = {sr, 0x0000};
		f.t.mab = 0x0453E;
		REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
		/* no EEM stop: straight to the sync */
		const Log want = identify_log() + Log{"ir:d0", "dr16:0000", "ir:28", "dr16:0000"} +
				 sync_tail(0x6904, sr);
		REQUIRE(f.t.log.size() >= want.size());
		CHECK_LOG(Log(f.t.log.begin(), f.t.log.begin() + static_cast<long>(want.size())), want);
		CHECK_EQ(f.d.pc(), 0x0453Cu); /* MAB - 2 in a low-power mode */
		/* the CPU gets SR without CPUOFF and GIE; the context keeps all bits
		 * and gives them back on release, with the PC 2 lower (Note 1411) */
		CHECK(contains(f.t.log, write_reg(0x0082, sr & 0xFFE7u)));
		f.t.log.clear();
		f.d.detach(DetachAction::Run);
		CHECK(contains(f.t.log, write_reg(0x0082, sr)));
		CHECK(contains(f.t.log, set_pc(0x453A)));
	}
}

TEST(debugger_attach_when_the_eem_has_already_stopped_the_cpu)
{
	Fx f;
	f.t.eem_control_script = {0x0080};
	f.t.captured = {0x0008, 0x0000};
	REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
	const Log want = identify_log() + Log{"ir:d0", "dr16:0000"} + sync_tail(0x6904, 0x0008);
	REQUIRE(f.t.log.size() >= want.size());
	CHECK_LOG(Log(f.t.log.begin(), f.t.log.begin() + static_cast<long>(want.size())), want);
	CHECK(f.t.eem_writes.size() == 9u); /* only the trigger clearing */
}

TEST(debugger_attach_clocks_through_the_wait_signal)
{
	Fx f;
	f.running_cpu();
	/* Note 805: the control-signal read after 0x1501 shows "wait" twice.
	 * The first five reads are the lock check (3), the CPUOFF check and
	 * the sync. */
	f.t.cntrl_sig_script = {0x0301, 0x0301, 0x0301, 0x0301, 0x0301,
				0x0309, 0x0309, 0x0301};
	REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(contains(f.t.log, Log{"ir:c8", "dr16:1501", "ir:28", "dr16:0000",
				    "tclk:0", "tclk:1", "ir:28", "dr16:0000",
				    "tclk:0", "tclk:1", "ir:28", "dr16:0000",
				    "ir:28", "tclk:0", "dr16:0000", "tclk:1"}));
}

TEST(debugger_attach_reads_the_pc_from_the_reset_vector_at_fffe)
{
	Fx f;
	f.t.mab = 0x00002; /* MAB - 4 = 0xFFFFE: Note 1495, 1637 */
	REQUIRE(f.attach() == Status::Ok);
	CHECK_EQ(f.d.pc(), 0x441Au);
	CHECK(contains(f.t.log, Log{"ir:21", "dr20:00000", "ir:28", "dr16:0000"} + read_word(0xFFFE)));
}

TEST(debugger_attach_waits_for_the_flash_controller)
{
	Fx f;
	f.running_cpu();
	f.t.memory[0x0144] = 0x9659; /* BUSY */
	f.t.millis_step = 30;
	REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
	/* polled until the 100 ms limit, never written */
	size_t reads = 0;
	for (size_t i = 0; i + 2 < f.t.log.size(); ++i) {
		if (f.t.log[i] == "dr20:00144") {
			++reads;
		}
	}
	CHECK(reads >= 3u);
	for (const auto &w : f.t.mem_writes) {
		CHECK(w.first != 0x0144u);
	}
}

TEST(debugger_attach_refuses_a_watchdog_without_its_password)
{
	Fx f;
	f.running_cpu();
	f.t.memory[0x015C] = 0x0000; /* link fault: not a WDTCTL read */
	CHECK(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::TargetError);
	CHECK(f.t.mem_writes.empty());
	CHECK(!f.d.attached());
	REQUIRE(f.t.log.size() >= 4u);
	CHECK_LOG(Log(f.t.log.end() - 4, f.t.log.end()),
		  (Log{"ir:30", "dr16:0006", "ir:a8", "close"}));
}

TEST(debugger_attach_without_sync_releases_and_writes_nothing)
{
	Fx f;
	f.running_cpu();
	f.t.cntrl_sig = 0x0101; /* sync bit never set */
	CHECK(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::TargetError);
	CHECK(f.t.mem_writes.empty());
	CHECK(!f.d.attached());
	REQUIRE(f.t.log.size() >= 4u);
	CHECK_LOG(Log(f.t.log.end() - 4, f.t.log.end()),
		  (Log{"ir:30", "dr16:0006", "ir:a8", "close"}));
}

TEST(debugger_attach_when_the_pipe_never_empties)
{
	Fx f;
	f.running_cpu();
	f.t.cntrl_sig = 0x0201; /* synchronized, CPUSUSP never set */
	CHECK(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::TargetError);
	CHECK(f.t.mem_writes.empty());
	REQUIRE(!f.t.log.empty());
	CHECK(f.t.log.back() == "close");
}

TEST(debugger_attach_passes_on_identify_failures)
{
	{
		Fx f;
		f.t.jtag_id = 0xFF;
		CHECK(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::NoTarget);
		CHECK(!f.d.attached());
	}
	{
		Fx f;
		f.t.cntrl_sig_script = {0x5555};
		CHECK(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Locked);
		CHECK(!f.d.attached());
		CHECK(f.t.mem_writes.empty());
	}
}

TEST(debugger_detach_restores_the_context_and_lets_the_program_run)
{
	Fx f;
	f.attached();
	f.d.detach(DetachAction::Run);
	CHECK_LOG(f.t.log, clear_triggers() + restore_release(0x4520, 0x0008, 0x6904, 0x0006) +
				   Log{"close"});
	CHECK(!f.d.attached());
	/* the watchdog gets its original bits back, without HOLD */
	REQUIRE(!f.t.mem_writes.empty());
	CHECK_EQ(f.t.mem_writes.back().second, 0x5A04);
}

TEST(debugger_detach_with_reset)
{
	Fx f;
	f.attached();
	f.d.detach(DetachAction::Reset);
	CHECK_LOG(f.t.log, clear_triggers() + Log{"ir:30", "dr16:0006", "ir:c8", "dr16:0c01",
						  "dr16:0401", "ir:a8", "close"});
	CHECK(!f.d.attached());
}
```

In `tests/host/CMakeLists.txt`, add after `test_fake_tap.cpp`:

```cmake
  test_debugger.cpp
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the interface**

Create `lib/msp430/include/msp430/debug.hpp` (the whole interface; the operations not defined in this task follow in Tasks 6–11):

```cpp
#pragma once
/* MSP430 CPUXv2 debugging over a Tap: attach with or without reset, the
 * saved CPU context, registers, memory while halted, single step, run with
 * EEM breakpoints, stop detection and detach. Port of the CPUXv2 macros of
 * TI's MSP Debug Stack (MSPDS 3.15.1.1, Bios/src/hal/macros). Device
 * parameters are those of the MSP430F5529 (JTAG ID 0x91).
 *
 * The debugger never erases or programs flash: it uses only identify(),
 * in_full_emulation() and disconnect() of Cpuxv2, none of the funclet,
 * erase or flash-write code.
 */
#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>

#include "msp430/cpuxv2.hpp"
#include "msp430/protocol.hpp"
#include "msp430/tap.hpp"

namespace msp430 {

class Debugger {
public:
	Debugger(Tap &tap, Cpuxv2 &cpu) : tap_(tap), cpu_(cpu) {}

	/* Take the CPU under JTAG control (normal entry: RST stays high).
	 * Running: SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2, the program stops where
	 * it is (stop reason Request). Reset: SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2,
	 * the CPU stops at the reset vector (stop reason Reset). Then the device
	 * ID is read and all EEM triggers are cleared. Statuses as
	 * Cpuxv2::connect. On failure the pins are released; a failure after
	 * JTAG took the CPU releases it with nothing written. */
	Status attach(AttachMode mode, uint32_t slot_hz, Identity &id);
	/* Clear the triggers, then Run: restore the context and let the program
	 * continue where it stopped; Reset: power-on reset and run. Halts a
	 * running CPU first. Releases the pins. Does nothing when not attached. */
	void detach(DetachAction action);

	bool attached() const { return attached_; }
	bool running() const { return running_; }
	/* Valid while attached and halted. */
	uint32_t pc() const { return ctx_.pc; }
	StopReason stop_reason() const { return reason_; }
	/* Index of the breakpoint at the stop address, or kNoBreakpoint. */
	uint8_t breakpoint_hit() const { return bp_hit_; }

	/* Stop a running CPU (stop reason Request, or Breakpoint if the EEM had
	 * already stopped it). Ok when already halted. */
	Status halt();
	/* Restore the context and run with the EEM and the breakpoints armed. */
	Status resume();
	/* While running: has the EEM stopped the CPU? If so the context is saved
	 * (stop reason Breakpoint). `halted` reports the state afterwards. */
	Status poll(bool &halted);
	/* Execute one instruction (stop reason Step). Timeout if the CPU did not
	 * stop by itself; it is halted anyway. */
	Status step();

	/* R0 (PC) and R2 (SR) come from the saved context, R3 reads 0. Values
	 * are 20 bits wide (SR 16). */
	Status read_regs(uint32_t regs[16]);
	/* R0 and R2 change the saved context, which is written back on release.
	 * R3 cannot be written. */
	Status write_reg(unsigned index, uint32_t value);
	/* Halted only. Little-endian byte pairs, one per word. The CPU context
	 * is preserved. TargetError if the CPU is no longer under JTAG control
	 * afterwards. Address checks are the caller's (Session). */
	Status read_mem(uint32_t addr, size_t nwords, uint8_t *dst);
	Status write_mem(uint32_t addr, size_t nwords, const uint8_t *src);

	/* One instruction-fetch trigger per breakpoint, index < kHwBreakpoints.
	 * Halted only. */
	Status set_breakpoint(unsigned index, uint32_t addr);
	/* kNoBreakpoint clears all trigger blocks. */
	Status clear_breakpoint(unsigned index);

private:
	struct Context {
		uint32_t pc = 0;
		uint16_t sr = 0;     /* as read, before CPUOFF and GIE were cleared */
		uint16_t wdtctl = 0; /* WDTCTL as read (0x69xx) */
		uint16_t irq = 0;    /* CNTRL_SIG_INTREQ at the sync */
	};

	Status sync_conditional();
	Status sync_assert_por();
	bool sync_jtag();
	bool wait_for_sync();
	bool hold_watchdog();
	void save_status_register();
	void wait_flash_idle();
	void restore_and_release(uint16_t eem_control);
	void abandon();
	void clear_triggers();
	void clear_jmb_out();

	uint16_t read_word(uint32_t addr);
	void write_word(uint32_t addr, uint16_t data);
	uint32_t read_cpu_reg(uint16_t mova);
	void write_cpu_reg(uint16_t mova, uint16_t data);
	void set_pc(uint32_t pc);
	uint16_t read_control();
	void write_control(uint16_t value);
	uint32_t eem_read(uint8_t reg);
	void eem_write(uint8_t reg, uint32_t value);

	Tap &tap_;
	Cpuxv2 &cpu_;
	Context ctx_;
	bool attached_ = false;
	bool running_ = false;
	StopReason reason_ = StopReason::None;
	uint8_t bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
	uint32_t bp_addr_[kHwBreakpoints] = {};
	uint32_t bp_used_ = 0; /* bit i: trigger block i is a breakpoint */
};

} // namespace msp430
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the port**

Create `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`. `attach()` accepts only `AttachMode::Running` until Task 6 adds the reset path, and `detach()` does not yet handle a running CPU (Task 10).

```cpp
/*
 * MSP430 CPUXv2 debugging: a port of the CPUXv2 macros of TI's MSP Debug
 * Stack, MSPDS-OPEN-SOURCE 3.15.1.1 (MSPDebugStack_OS_Package_3_15_1_1.zip),
 * Bios/src/hal/macros and the helpers they inline from Bios/src/hil/edt.h.
 * The macro or helper is named above each port, with its lines. The JTAG ID
 * 0x91 paths are taken; the device parameters are those of the MSP430F5529
 * in TI's device database (MSPDS 3.3.1.3 TemplateDeviceDb): no LPMx.5 test
 * register settings (DisableLpmx5() does nothing), altRomAddressForCpuRead
 * set (feature "_1377"), "large" EEM with 8 memory-bus triggers.
 *
 * Deliberate differences from MSPDS:
 *  - a sync that does not complete (SyncJtagXv2, wait_for_synch), a CPU pipe
 *    that never empties, or a JTAG ID other than 0x91 where MSPDS reads one
 *    ends the operation with TargetError; before the context is saved the
 *    CPU is then released without anything written (MSPDS carries on);
 *  - WDTCTL is checked for its read password 0x69 before it is written, not
 *    afterwards as the DLL does (WatchdogControl::checkRead);
 *  - "check for Init State" after a sync is Cpuxv2::in_full_emulation(),
 *    which also checks the JTAG ID; read_mem, write_mem, read_regs and
 *    write_reg end with the same check;
 *  - before the watchdog is held, the sync waits up to 100 ms for the flash
 *    controller to finish an erase or write the firmware had started
 *    (FCTL3.BUSY; not in MSPDS);
 *  - SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2 leaves out the per-module clock
 *    control (ETKEYSEL/ETCLKSEL), whose module codes come from the DLL's
 *    device database; the modules keep their reset defaults. GENCLKCTRL ends
 *    at 0x000E as in MSPDS: MCLK, SMCLK and ACLK stop while the CPU is
 *    halted;
 *  - SingleStepXv2 polls up to 496 times as MSPDS does (its counter starts
 *    at 4); if the CPU has not stopped by then, it is stopped as halt()
 *    does, trigger block 0 is restored and Timeout returned (MSPDS returns
 *    an error and leaves trigger block 0 set up for the step);
 *  - breakpoints: one trigger block per breakpoint, set up as the trigger
 *    SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2 uses for "next instruction fetch",
 *    with the exact address; the value register is read back to confirm
 *    that the trigger block exists. All trigger blocks are cleared on
 *    attach and detach.
 *
 * Copyright (C) 2012 Texas Instruments Incorporated - http://www.ti.com/
 *
 *  Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without
 *  modification, are permitted provided that the following conditions
 *  are met:
 *
 *    Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright
 *    notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer.
 *
 *    Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above copyright
 *    notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer in the
 *    documentation and/or other materials provided with the
 *    distribution.
 *
 *    Neither the name of Texas Instruments Incorporated nor the names of
 *    its contributors may be used to endorse or promote products derived
 *    from this software without specific prior written permission.
 *
 *  THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS AND CONTRIBUTORS
 *  "AS IS" AND ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT
 *  LIMITED TO, THE IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR
 *  A PARTICULAR PURPOSE ARE DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE COPYRIGHT
 *  OWNER OR CONTRIBUTORS BE LIABLE FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL,
 *  SPECIAL, EXEMPLARY, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES (INCLUDING, BUT NOT
 *  LIMITED TO, PROCUREMENT OF SUBSTITUTE GOODS OR SERVICES; LOSS OF USE,
 *  DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS INTERRUPTION) HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY
 *  THEORY OF LIABILITY, WHETHER IN CONTRACT, STRICT LIABILITY, OR TORT
 *  (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE) ARISING IN ANY WAY OUT OF THE USE
 *  OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGE.
 */
#include "msp430/debug.hpp"

namespace msp430 {

namespace {

/* Device registers (F5xx) */
constexpr uint16_t kWdtCtl = 0x015C;
constexpr uint16_t kWdtHold = 0x5A80;    /* WDTPW | WDTHOLD (DLL WatchdogControl) */
constexpr uint16_t kWdtPw = 0x5A00;
constexpr uint8_t kWdtReadPw = 0x69;
constexpr uint16_t kFctl3 = 0x0144;
constexpr uint16_t kFctl3Busy = 0x0001;
constexpr uint32_t kFlashIdleTimeoutMs = 100;
constexpr uint16_t kSfrIfg1 = 0x0102;
constexpr uint16_t kVmaIfg = 0x0008;
constexpr uint16_t kSafePc = 0x0004;      /* SAFE_PC_ADDRESS (JTAG_defs.h) */
constexpr uint16_t kAltRomAddr = 0x0FF6;  /* ReadCpuRegXv2, altRomAddressForCpuRead */
constexpr uint16_t kResetVector = 0xFFFE;
constexpr uint16_t kSrCpuOff = 0x0010;
constexpr uint16_t kSrKeepMask = 0xFFE7;  /* SR with CPUOFF and GIE cleared */

/* Control signal register bits (JTAG_defs.h) */
constexpr uint16_t kSigHalt = 0x0002;
constexpr uint16_t kSigIntReq = 0x0004;
constexpr uint16_t kSigWait = 0x0008;     /* "Note 805: check if wait is set" */
constexpr uint16_t kSigCpuOff = 0x0020;
constexpr uint16_t kSigCpuSusp = 0x0100;
constexpr uint16_t kSigSync = 0x0200;

/* EEM (EEM_defs.h) */
constexpr uint16_t kEemEn = 0x0001;
constexpr uint16_t kClearStop = 0x0002;
constexpr uint16_t kEmuClkEn = 0x0004;
constexpr uint16_t kEmuFeatEn = 0x0008;
constexpr uint16_t kEemStopped = 0x0080;
constexpr uint8_t kMbTrigVal = 0x00;
constexpr uint8_t kMbTrigCtl = 0x02;
constexpr uint8_t kMbTrigMsk = 0x04;
constexpr uint8_t kMbTrigCmb = 0x06;
constexpr uint8_t kTriggerBlockSize = 0x08;
constexpr uint8_t kBreakReact = 0x80;
constexpr uint8_t kGenClkCtrl = 0x88;
constexpr uint8_t kEemRead = 0x01;
constexpr uint32_t kCmpEqualFetchMab = 0x0000; /* CMP_EQUAL + TRIG_0 + MAB */
constexpr uint32_t kMaskAll = 0xFFFFF;
constexpr uint32_t kNoMask = 0x00000;
constexpr uint32_t kStopClocks = 0x000E;    /* *_SEL0 + STOP_MCLK + STOP_SMCLK + STOP_ACLK */
constexpr uint32_t kJclkStopClocks = 0x7F8E; /* *_SEL3 + STOP_MCLK + STOP_SMCLK + STOP_ACLK */

/* Loop limits, as in MSPDS */
constexpr unsigned kEemStopPolls = 3000;
constexpr unsigned kWaitPolls = 30000;
constexpr unsigned kPipePolls = 10000;
constexpr unsigned kSyncPolls = 50;
constexpr unsigned kStepPolls = 496;
constexpr uint32_t kPorDelayMs = 40;

constexpr uint16_t mova_to_abs(unsigned reg) /* MOVA Rn, &abs */
{
	return static_cast<uint16_t>(0x0060u | ((reg << 8) & 0x0F00u));
}

constexpr uint16_t mova_imm(unsigned reg, uint32_t value) /* MOVA #imm20, Rn */
{
	return static_cast<uint16_t>(0x0080u | ((value >> 8) & 0x0F00u) | (reg & 0x000Fu));
}

} // namespace

/* ---- JTAG helpers (Bios/src/hil/edt.h) ---- */

/* ReadMemWordXv2, edt.h:369-383 */
uint16_t Debugger::read_word(uint32_t addr)
{
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddr16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift20(addr & 0xFFFFFu);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrDataCapture);
	const uint16_t data = tap_.dr_shift16(0);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	return data;
}

/* WriteMemWordXv2, edt.h:416-432 */
void Debugger::write_word(uint32_t addr, uint16_t data)
{
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0500);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddr16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift20(addr);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrDataToAddr);
	tap_.dr_shift16(data);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0501);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
}

/* ReadCpuRegXv2, edt.h:541-589, with altRomAddressForCpuRead set: the CPU
 * executes MOVA Rn, &0x0FF6 and the value is captured from the data bus. */
uint32_t Debugger::read_cpu_reg(uint16_t mova)
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture); /* jtagId: selects the JMB address, unused here */
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrData16Bit);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(mova);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x1401);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrData16Bit);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(kAltRomAddr);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x3FFD);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0501);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrDataCapture);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	const uint16_t low = tap_.dr_shift16(0);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	const uint16_t high = tap_.dr_shift16(0);
	for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
		tap_.clr_tclk();
		tap_.set_tclk();
	}
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrDataCapture);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	return (static_cast<uint32_t>(high) << 16) + low;
}

/* WriteCpuRegXv2, edt.h:651-675 */
void Debugger::write_cpu_reg(uint16_t mova, uint16_t data)
{
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrData16Bit);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(mova);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x1401);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrData16Bit);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(data);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x3FFD);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddrCapture);
	tap_.dr_shift20(0x00000);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0501);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrDataCapture);
	tap_.set_tclk();
}

/* SetPcXv2(0x0080 | (pc >> 8 & 0x0F00), pc & 0xFFFF), edt.h:728-749 */
void Debugger::set_pc(uint32_t pc)
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0000);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrData16Bit);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(mova_imm(0, pc));
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x1400);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrData16Bit);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(static_cast<uint16_t>(pc & 0xFFFFu));
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x4303);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddrCapture);
	tap_.dr_shift20(0x00000);
}

/* SyncJtagXv2, edt.h:804-816. MSPDS ignores a sync that never comes. */
bool Debugger::sync_jtag()
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x1501);
	if (tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture) != kJtagId91) {
		return false;
	}
	for (unsigned i = 0; i < kSyncPolls; ++i) {
		const uint16_t sig = tap_.dr_shift16(0x0000);
		if (sig != 0xFFFF && (sig & kSigSync) != 0) {
			return true;
		}
	}
	return false;
}

/* eem_read_control() + SetReg_16Bits(0) */
uint16_t Debugger::read_control()
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexReadControl);
	return tap_.dr_shift16(0x0000);
}

/* eem_write_control() + SetReg_16Bits(value) */
void Debugger::write_control(uint16_t value)
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexWriteControl);
	tap_.dr_shift16(value);
}

/* EemDataExchangeXv2.c:66-86, one read; IR_EMEX_DATA_EXCHANGE32 must be
 * selected. */
uint32_t Debugger::eem_read(uint8_t reg)
{
	tap_.dr_shift32(static_cast<uint32_t>(reg | kEemRead));
	return tap_.dr_shift32(0);
}

/* EemDataExchangeXv2.c:88-106, one write */
void Debugger::eem_write(uint8_t reg, uint32_t value)
{
	tap_.dr_shift32(reg);
	tap_.dr_shift32(value);
}

/* ---- context save and restore ---- */

/* Not in MSPDS: release a CPU whose context was not saved, without writing
 * anything to the target, and let go of the pins. */
void Debugger::abandon()
{
	write_control(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigRelease);
	tap_.close();
	attached_ = false;
	running_ = false;
}

/* Not in MSPDS (see the file header). */
void Debugger::wait_flash_idle()
{
	const uint32_t start = tap_.millis();
	while ((read_word(kFctl3) & kFctl3Busy) != 0) {
		if (tap_.millis() - start >= kFlashIdleTimeoutMs) {
			break;
		}
	}
}

/* SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2.c:302-308 /
 * SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2.c:154-159: hold the watchdog, keep its
 * other bits. */
bool Debugger::hold_watchdog()
{
	const uint16_t wdt = read_word(kWdtCtl);
	if ((wdt >> 8) != kWdtReadPw) {
		return false;
	}
	write_word(kWdtCtl, static_cast<uint16_t>(kWdtHold | (wdt & 0x00FFu)));
	ctx_.wdtctl = wdt;
	return true;
}

/* SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2.c:310-324: save SR, then clear CPUOFF
 * and GIE in the CPU so that it executes JTAG-fed instructions. */
void Debugger::save_status_register()
{
	ctx_.sr = static_cast<uint16_t>(read_cpu_reg(mova_to_abs(2)));
	write_cpu_reg(mova_imm(2, 0), static_cast<uint16_t>(ctx_.sr & kSrKeepMask));
}

/* SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2.c:70-329 */
Status Debugger::sync_conditional()
{
	/* DisableLpmx5(): no LPMx.5 test-register settings on the F5529. */
	if ((read_control() & kEemStopped) == 0) {
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
		if ((tap_.dr_shift16(0x0000) & kSigCpuOff) == 0) {
			/* Not in a low-power mode: let the EEM stop the CPU on the
			 * next instruction fetch, using trigger block 0. */
			tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
			const uint32_t tb_val = eem_read(kMbTrigVal);
			const uint32_t tb_ctl = eem_read(kMbTrigCtl);
			const uint32_t tb_msk = eem_read(kMbTrigMsk);
			const uint32_t tb_cmb = eem_read(kMbTrigCmb);
			const uint32_t tb_break = eem_read(kBreakReact);
			eem_write(kMbTrigCtl, kCmpEqualFetchMab);
			eem_write(kMbTrigMsk, kMaskAll);
			eem_write(kMbTrigCmb, 0x0001); /* EN0 */
			eem_write(kBreakReact, 0x0001);
			eem_write(kGenClkCtrl, kStopClocks);
			write_control(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop | kEemEn);
			for (unsigned i = 0; i < kEemStopPolls; ++i) {
				if ((read_control() & kEemStopped) != 0) {
					break;
				}
			}
			tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
			eem_write(kMbTrigVal, tb_val);
			eem_write(kMbTrigCtl, tb_ctl);
			eem_write(kMbTrigMsk, tb_msk);
			eem_write(kMbTrigCmb, tb_cmb);
			eem_write(kBreakReact, tb_break);
		}
	}

	write_control(kEmuClkEn | kEemEn); /* clock control before sync */
	if (!sync_jtag()) {
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	/* reset the CPU stop reaction: the CPU is under JTAG control now */
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexWriteControl);
	tap_.dr_shift16(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop | kEemEn);
	tap_.dr_shift16(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop);

	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x1501);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
	uint16_t sig = tap_.dr_shift16(0);
	for (unsigned n = 0; (sig & kSigWait) != 0 && n < kWaitPolls; ++n) {
		tap_.clr_tclk();
		tap_.set_tclk();
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
		sig = tap_.dr_shift16(0);
	}

	/* clock the CPU into full emulation state: wait for CPUSUSP */
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
	bool pipe_empty = false;
	for (unsigned n = 0; !pipe_empty && n < kPipePolls; ++n) {
		tap_.clr_tclk();
		pipe_empty = (tap_.dr_shift16(0x0000) & kSigCpuSusp) != 0;
		tap_.set_tclk();
	}
	if (!pipe_empty) {
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0501);

	/* i_SaveContext */
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddrCapture);
	const uint32_t mab = tap_.dr_shift20(0);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
	sig = tap_.dr_shift16(0);
	const bool cpuoff = (sig & kSigCpuOff) != 0;
	ctx_.irq = static_cast<uint16_t>(sig & kSigIntReq);
	uint32_t pc = (mab - (cpuoff ? 2u : 4u)) & 0xFFFFFu;
	if ((pc & 0xFFFFu) == 0xFFFEu) { /* Note 1495, 1637 */
		pc = read_word(kResetVector);
	}

	write_control(kEmuFeatEn | kEmuClkEn | kClearStop);
	if (!cpu_.in_full_emulation()) { /* MSPDS: "check for Init State", unchecked */
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	wait_flash_idle();
	if (!hold_watchdog()) {
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	ctx_.pc = pc;
	save_status_register();
	return Status::Ok;
}

/* RestoreContext_ReleaseJtagXv2.c:69-196 with mdb = 0 (no software
 * breakpoints), releaseJtag = 0 and ulpDebug = 0. */
void Debugger::restore_and_release(uint16_t eem_control)
{
	write_cpu_reg(mova_imm(2, 0), ctx_.sr);
	write_word(kWdtCtl, static_cast<uint16_t>(kWdtPw | (ctx_.wdtctl & 0x00FFu)));
	uint32_t pc = ctx_.pc;
	if ((ctx_.sr & kSrCpuOff) != 0) { /* Note 1411 */
		pc = (pc - 2u) & 0xFFFFFu;
	}
	set_pc(pc);
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0401);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddrCapture);
	/* one more clock to advance the CPU, or enabling the EEM stops it again */
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	if ((tap_.dr_shift16(0) & kSigHalt) != 0) {
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
		tap_.dr_shift16(0x0403);
	}
	tap_.set_tclk();
	write_control(eem_control);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture); /* id; DisableLpmx5() does nothing here */
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigRelease);
}

void Debugger::clear_triggers()
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
	for (unsigned i = 0; i < kHwBreakpoints; ++i) {
		eem_write(static_cast<uint8_t>(i * kTriggerBlockSize + kMbTrigCmb), 0);
	}
	eem_write(kBreakReact, 0);
	bp_used_ = 0;
}

/* ---- public operations ---- */

Status Debugger::attach(AttachMode mode, uint32_t slot_hz, Identity &id)
{
	if (mode != AttachMode::Running) {
		return Status::BadArg; /* AttachMode::Reset comes with sync_assert_por() */
	}
	detach(DetachAction::Run);
	const Status st = cpu_.identify(EntryMode::Normal, slot_hz, id);
	if (st != Status::Ok) {
		return st;
	}
	attached_ = true;
	running_ = false;
	const Status sync = sync_conditional();
	if (sync != Status::Ok) {
		return sync;
	}
	id.device_id = read_word(id.device_id_ptr + 4u);
	clear_triggers();
	if (!cpu_.in_full_emulation()) {
		detach(DetachAction::Run);
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	reason_ = StopReason::Request;
	bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
	return Status::Ok;
}

void Debugger::detach(DetachAction action)
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return;
	}
	clear_triggers();
	if (action == DetachAction::Reset) {
		write_control(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop);
		cpu_.disconnect(); /* ReleaseDevice_430Xv2(V_RESET), pins released */
	} else {
		restore_and_release(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop); /* DLL run(): EEM off */
		tap_.close();
	}
	attached_ = false;
	running_ = false;
	reason_ = StopReason::None;
}

} // namespace msp430
```

```cpp
namespace {

/* SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2.c:68-228, JTAG ID 0x91, sync at the
 * first look, SFRIFG1 without the vacant-memory flag. */
Log assert_por_log(unsigned wdt)
{
	return Log{ir(0x28), ir(0xB0)} + eem_write(0x88, 0x7F8E) +
	       Log{ir(0x30), d16(0x0005), ir(0xC8), d16(0x1501), ir(0x28), d16(0)} + kTclk +
	       Log{ir(0xC8), d16(0x0C01), "delay:40", d16(0x0401), ir(0x82)} + kTclk + kTclk +
	       Log{d16(0x0004)} + kTclk + kTclk + Log{ir(0x42)} + kTclk + kTclk +
	       Log{ir(0xC8), d16(0x0501)} + kTclk + Log{ir(0x30), d16(0x000E), ir(0x28), d16(0)} +
	       read_word(0x015C) + write_word(0x015C, 0x5A80u | (wdt & 0xFFu)) +
	       Log{ir(0x21), d20(0)} + read_word(0xFFFE) + Log{ir(0xB0)} +
	       eem_write(0x88, 0x000E) + read_word(0x0102);
}
```

In `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`, add before the last line, `} // namespace msp430`:

```cpp
/* wait_for_synch, edt.h:819-830 (50 polls; MSPDS reads up to 51 times but
 * fails on the 51st anyway). */
bool Debugger::wait_for_sync()
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture);
	for (unsigned i = 0; i < kSyncPolls; ++i) {
		if ((tap_.dr_shift16(0) & kSigSync) != 0) {
			return true;
		}
	}
	return false;
}

/* SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2.c:68-228, JTAG ID 0x91 */
Status Debugger::sync_assert_por()
{
	if (tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture) != kJtagId91) {
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	/* switch all functional clocks to JCK = 1 and stop them */
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
	eem_write(kGenClkCtrl, kJclkStopClocks);
	write_control(kEmuClkEn | kEemEn);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x1501);
	if (!wait_for_sync()) {
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	tap_.clr_tclk(); /* one more clock to empty the pipe */
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0C01); /* release CPUFLUSH, apply POR */
	tap_.delay_ms(kPorDelayMs);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0401); /* release POR */
	/* force the PC to a safe address (JMP $) */
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrData16Bit);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.dr_shift16(kSafePc);
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk(); /* JTAG ID 0x91: one more */
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrDataCapture);
	/* two more to release the CPU-internal POR delay signals */
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
	tap_.dr_shift16(0x0501); /* set CPUFLUSH */
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	tap_.set_tclk();
	write_control(kEmuFeatEn | kEmuClkEn | kClearStop);
	if (!cpu_.in_full_emulation() || !hold_watchdog()) {
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddrCapture); /* MAB; JTAG ID 0x91 takes the reset vector */
	tap_.dr_shift20(0);
	ctx_.pc = read_word(kResetVector);
	ctx_.sr = 0;
	ctx_.irq = 0;
	/* module clock control (ETKEYSEL/ETCLKSEL): not ported, see the header */
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
	eem_write(kGenClkCtrl, kStopClocks);
	/* JTAG ID 0x91: reset the vacant-memory flag in SFRIFG1 */
	const uint16_t sfr = read_word(kSfrIfg1);
	if ((sfr & kVmaIfg) != 0) {
		set_pc(kSafePc);
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
		tap_.dr_shift16(0x0501);
		tap_.set_tclk();
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddrCapture);
		write_word(kSfrIfg1, static_cast<uint16_t>(sfr & ~kVmaIfg));
	}
	return Status::Ok;
}
```

Replace the whole `Debugger::attach` function with:

```cpp
Status Debugger::attach(AttachMode mode, uint32_t slot_hz, Identity &id)
{
	detach(DetachAction::Run);
	const Status st = cpu_.identify(EntryMode::Normal, slot_hz, id);
	if (st != Status::Ok) {
		return st;
	}
	attached_ = true;
	running_ = false;
	const Status sync = (mode == AttachMode::Reset) ? sync_assert_por() : sync_conditional();
	if (sync != Status::Ok) {
		return sync;
	}
	id.device_id = read_word(id.device_id_ptr + 4u);
	clear_triggers();
	if (!cpu_.in_full_emulation()) {
		detach(DetachAction::Run);
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	reason_ = (mode == AttachMode::Reset) ? StopReason::Reset : StopReason::Request;
	bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
	return Status::Ok;
}
```

In `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`, add before the last line, `} // namespace msp430`:

```cpp
/* i_ReadJmbOut, Bios/src/hil/edt.c:44-57 */
void Debugger::clear_jmb_out()
{
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrJmbExchange);
	if ((tap_.dr_shift16(0x0000) & kJmbOut1Rdy) != 0) {
		tap_.dr_shift16(kJmb32Bit | kJmbOutReq);
		tap_.dr_shift16(0);
		tap_.dr_shift16(0);
	}
}

/* ReadAllCpuRegsXv2.c:58-91, JTAG ID 0x91 */
Status Debugger::read_regs(uint32_t regs[16])
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	regs[0] = ctx_.pc;
	regs[2] = ctx_.sr;
	regs[3] = 0;
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture); /* id */
	for (unsigned r = 1; r < 16; ++r) {
		if (r == 2) {
			r += 2;
		}
		regs[r] = read_cpu_reg(mova_to_abs(r)) & 0xFFFFFu;
		/* JTAG ID 0x91: set the PC to a safe address */
		set_pc(kSafePc);
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSig16Bit);
		tap_.dr_shift16(0x0501);
		tap_.set_tclk();
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddrCapture);
	}
	clear_jmb_out();
	return cpu_.in_full_emulation() ? Status::Ok : Status::TargetError;
}

/* WriteAllCpuRegsXv2.c:60-85, for one register */
Status Debugger::write_reg(unsigned index, uint32_t value)
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	if (index > 15 || index == 3 || value >= kAddressSpace ||
	    (index == 0 && (value & 1u) != 0) || (index == 2 && value > 0xFFFFu)) {
		return Status::BadArg;
	}
	if (index == 0) {
		ctx_.pc = value;
		return Status::Ok;
	}
	if (index == 2) {
		ctx_.sr = static_cast<uint16_t>(value);
		return Status::Ok;
	}
	write_cpu_reg(mova_imm(index, value), static_cast<uint16_t>(value));
	return cpu_.in_full_emulation() ? Status::Ok : Status::TargetError;
}
```

### Task 8: `Debugger`: memory while halted

Port of `ReadMemWordsXv2.c` (lines 57–83) and `WriteMemWordsXv2.c` (lines 58–104). Unlike Phase A's `ReadMemQuick` they do not load the PC, so the saved context stays valid. Both end with the full-emulation check Phase A added, so a link that died during the transfer is reported. The address rules (what a read-only session may write) are `Session`'s, in Task 13.

```cpp
/* ReadMemWordsXv2.c:57-83 */
Status Debugger::read_mem(uint32_t addr, size_t nwords, uint8_t *dst)
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	tap_.clr_tclk();
	for (size_t i = 0; i < nwords; ++i) {
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrAddr16Bit);
		tap_.dr_shift20(addr + 2u * static_cast<uint32_t>(i));
		tap_.set_tclk();
		tap_.clr_tclk();
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrDataCapture);
		const uint16_t word = tap_.dr_shift16(0);
		dst[2 * i] = static_cast<uint8_t>(word);
		dst[2 * i + 1] = static_cast<uint8_t>(word >> 8);
	}
	tap_.set_tclk();
	tap_.clr_tclk(); /* one more cycle, so the CPU drives the correct MAB */
	tap_.set_tclk();
	return cpu_.in_full_emulation() ? Status::Ok : Status::TargetError;
}

/* WriteMemWordsXv2.c:58-104; its loop body is WriteMemWordXv2. */
Status Debugger::write_mem(uint32_t addr, size_t nwords, const uint8_t *src)
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	if (!cpu_.in_full_emulation()) {
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	for (size_t i = 0; i < nwords; ++i) {
		write_word(addr + 2u * static_cast<uint32_t>(i),
			   static_cast<uint16_t>(src[2 * i] | (src[2 * i + 1] << 8)));
	}
	return cpu_.in_full_emulation() ? Status::Ok : Status::TargetError;
}
```

### Task 9: `Debugger`: breakpoints

One EEM trigger block per breakpoint, written through `EemDataExchangeXv2` (lines 62–106): value = address, control = `CMP_EQUAL + TRIG_0 + MAB` (instruction fetch on the address bus, `EEM_defs.h`), mask = none, combination register = bit *i*, and bit *i* in `BREAKREACT`. This is the trigger `SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2` sets up for "next instruction fetch" (lines 130–137) with the exact address instead of "any". The value register is read back: a trigger block that does not exist, or a dead link, ends the operation with `TargetError` instead of a breakpoint that silently never fires.

In `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`, add before the last line, `} // namespace msp430`:

```cpp
Status Debugger::set_breakpoint(unsigned index, uint32_t addr)
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	if (index >= kHwBreakpoints || addr >= kAddressSpace || (addr & 1u) != 0) {
		return Status::BadArg;
	}
	const uint8_t tb = static_cast<uint8_t>(index * kTriggerBlockSize);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
	eem_write(static_cast<uint8_t>(tb + kMbTrigVal), addr);
	eem_write(static_cast<uint8_t>(tb + kMbTrigCtl), kCmpEqualFetchMab);
	eem_write(static_cast<uint8_t>(tb + kMbTrigMsk), kNoMask);
	eem_write(static_cast<uint8_t>(tb + kMbTrigCmb), 1u << index);
	if ((eem_read(static_cast<uint8_t>(tb + kMbTrigVal)) & 0xFFFFFu) != addr) {
		eem_write(static_cast<uint8_t>(tb + kMbTrigCmb), 0);
		return Status::TargetError; /* no such trigger block, or a dead link */
	}
	bp_addr_[index] = addr;
	bp_used_ |= 1u << index;
	eem_write(kBreakReact, bp_used_);
	return Status::Ok;
}

Status Debugger::clear_breakpoint(unsigned index)
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	if (index == kNoBreakpoint) {
		clear_triggers();
		return Status::Ok;
	}
	if (index >= kHwBreakpoints) {
		return Status::BadArg;
	}
	bp_used_ &= ~(1u << index);
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
	eem_write(static_cast<uint8_t>(index * kTriggerBlockSize + kMbTrigCmb), 0);
	eem_write(kBreakReact, bp_used_);
	return Status::Ok;
}
```

### Task 10: `Debugger`: resume, stop detection, halt

`resume()` is `RestoreContext_ReleaseJtagXv2` with EEM control 0x0007, as the DLL's `DebugManagerMSP430::run()` sends when breakpoints are polled. `poll()` is `WaitForEem.c` (lines 61–111) with the mask 0x80 the DLL's `PollingManager` uses, followed by the conditional sync on a stop (`DebugManagerMSP430::runEvent`, `BP_HIT_FLAG`). `halt()` looks first (a breakpoint may have stopped the CPU already) and otherwise uses the conditional sync, which stops the CPU through the EEM. `detach()` now halts a running CPU before it restores the context.

- Modify: `tests/host/test_debugger.cpp`

- [ ] **Step 3: Add run control**

In `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`, add before the last line, `} // namespace msp430`:

```cpp
Status Debugger::halt()
{
	bool halted = false;
	Status st = poll(halted);
	if (st != Status::Ok || halted) {
		return st;
	}
	st = sync_conditional();
	if (st != Status::Ok) {
		return st;
	}
	running_ = false;
	reason_ = StopReason::Request;
	bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
	return Status::Ok;
}

/* DebugManagerMSP430::run(): RestoreContext_ReleaseJtag with EEM control
 * 0x0007 when breakpoints are polled. */
Status Debugger::resume()
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	restore_and_release(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop | kEemEn);
	running_ = true;
	reason_ = StopReason::None;
	bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
	return Status::Ok;
}

/* WaitForEem.c:61-111 with the mask 0x80 the DLL's PollingManager passes,
 * then SyncJtag_Conditional_SaveContextXv2 as
 * DebugManagerMSP430::runEvent does on BP_HIT_FLAG. */
Status Debugger::poll(bool &halted)
{
	halted = !running_;
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (!running_) {
		return Status::Ok;
	}
	if (tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture) != kJtagId91) { /* jtagIdIsValid */
		abandon();
		return Status::TargetError;
	}
	if ((read_control() & kEemStopped) == 0) {
		return Status::Ok;
	}
	const Status st = sync_conditional();
	if (st != Status::Ok) {
		return st;
	}
	running_ = false;
	halted = true;
	reason_ = StopReason::Breakpoint;
	bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
	for (unsigned i = 0; i < kHwBreakpoints; ++i) {
		if ((bp_used_ & (1u << i)) != 0 && bp_addr_[i] == ctx_.pc) {
			bp_hit_ = static_cast<uint8_t>(i);
			break;
		}
	}
	return Status::Ok;
}
```

Replace the whole `Debugger::detach` function with:

```cpp
void Debugger::detach(DetachAction action)
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return;
	}
	if (running_ && halt() != Status::Ok) {
		return; /* halt() failed and has released the CPU */
	}
	clear_triggers();
	if (action == DetachAction::Reset) {
		write_control(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop);
		cpu_.disconnect(); /* ReleaseDevice_430Xv2(V_RESET), pins released */
	} else {
		restore_and_release(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop); /* DLL run(): EEM off */
		tap_.close();
	}
	attached_ = false;
	running_ = false;
	reason_ = StopReason::None;
}
```

### Task 11: `Debugger`: single step

Port of `SingleStepXv2.c` (lines 57–164): trigger block 0 and `BREAKREACT` are saved and set to "next instruction fetch" (the `MyIn` table, lines 62–72), the context is released with EEM control 0x0007, the EEM control register is polled for the stop, the CPU is taken again with the conditional sync and trigger block 0 is restored. A CPU that is off (low-power mode) without a pending interrupt executes nothing; it is released and taken again without a trigger (lines 147–162). Deliberate difference (also in the file header): when the stop does not come within the poll budget, MSPDS returns an error and leaves trigger block 0 set up; the port stops the CPU as `halt()` does, restores trigger block 0 and returns `Timeout`.

- Modify: `tests/host/test_debugger.cpp`
- Modify: `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`

```cpp
	for (int i = 0; i < 496 + 1; ++i) {

	CHECK(f.d.attached());

	REQUIRE(f.t.eem_writes.size() >= 5u);
	CHECK_EQ(f.t.eem_writes.back().first, 0x80);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the step**

In `lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp`, add before the last line, `} // namespace msp430`:

```cpp
/* SingleStepXv2.c:57-164 */
Status Debugger::step()
{
	if (!attached_) {
		return Status::BadState;
	}
	if (running_) {
		return Status::Running;
	}
	const bool use_eem = (ctx_.sr & kSrCpuOff) == 0 || (ctx_.irq & kSigIntReq) != 0;
	uint32_t saved[5] = {};
	if (use_eem) { /* trigger block 0 on the next instruction fetch */
		tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
		saved[0] = eem_read(kMbTrigVal);
		saved[1] = eem_read(kMbTrigCtl);
		saved[2] = eem_read(kMbTrigMsk);
		saved[3] = eem_read(kMbTrigCmb);
		saved[4] = eem_read(kBreakReact);
		eem_write(kMbTrigCtl, kCmpEqualFetchMab);
		eem_write(kMbTrigMsk, kMaskAll);
		eem_write(kMbTrigCmb, 0x0001);
		eem_write(kBreakReact, 0x0001);
	}
	restore_and_release(kEmuClkEn | kClearStop | kEemEn);
	running_ = true;

	if (!use_eem) {
		/* CPU off and no interrupt pending: nothing runs. */
		if (tap_.ir_shift(kIrCntrlSigCapture) != kJtagId91) { /* LPMx.5 */
			abandon();
			return Status::TargetError;
		}
		const Status st = sync_conditional();
		if (st != Status::Ok) {
			return st;
		}
		running_ = false;
		reason_ = StopReason::Step;
		bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
		return Status::Ok;
	}

	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexReadControl);
	bool stopped = false;
	for (unsigned n = 0; n < kStepPolls && !stopped; ++n) {
		stopped = (tap_.dr_shift16(0x0000) & kEemStopped) != 0;
	}
	const Status st = sync_conditional();
	if (st != Status::Ok) {
		return st;
	}
	running_ = false;
	tap_.ir_shift(kIrEmexDataExchange32);
	eem_write(kMbTrigVal, saved[0]);
	eem_write(kMbTrigCtl, saved[1]);
	eem_write(kMbTrigMsk, saved[2]);
	eem_write(kMbTrigCmb, saved[3]);
	eem_write(kBreakReact, saved[4]);
	bp_hit_ = kNoBreakpoint;
	if (!stopped) {
		reason_ = StopReason::Request;
		return Status::Timeout;
	}
	reason_ = StopReason::Step;
	return Status::Ok;
}
```

### Task 12: `Debugger`: cross-cutting checks and "never writes flash"

These tests check behaviour the earlier tasks built, across operations. They should pass at once. If one fails, fix the code, not the test, and note which earlier task missed it in your report. The last test is the spec's "no flash write" test: every operation, run against the fake, writes memory only at WDTCTL, SFRIFG1 and the RAM the test asks for, never to the flash controller (`FCTL1`–`FCTL4`, 0x0140–0x014F), and feeds no funclet through the mailbox. `debug.cpp` also has no reference to the funclet, erase or flash-write code of `Cpuxv2`; Step 3 checks that mechanically.

```cpp
TEST(debugger_operations_need_an_attached_cpu)
{
	Fx f;
	uint32_t regs[16] = {};
	uint8_t buf[2] = {};
	bool halted = false;
	CHECK(f.d.read_regs(regs) == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.write_reg(4, 0) == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.read_mem(0x2400, 1, buf) == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.write_mem(0x2400, 1, buf) == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.halt() == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.resume() == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.poll(halted) == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.step() == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.set_breakpoint(0, 0x4400) == Status::BadState);
	CHECK(f.d.clear_breakpoint(0) == Status::BadState);

}

	CHECK(f.d.write_reg(4, 0) == Status::Running);
	CHECK(f.d.read_mem(0x2400, 1, buf) == Status::Running);

TEST(debugger_attach_detaches_an_open_session_first)

	f.running_cpu();
	REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK_LOG(Log(f.t.log.begin(), f.t.log.begin() + static_cast<long>(clear_triggers().size())),
		  clear_triggers());

/* The debugger never starts a funclet and never writes the flash
 * controller or flash: every operation, run against the fake, writes
 * memory only at WDTCTL, SFRIFG1 and the RAM address the test asks for. */
TEST(debugger_never_writes_flash)
{
	Fx f;
	f.t.memory[0x0102] = 0x0008;
	REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Reset, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
	f.d.detach(DetachAction::Run);
	REQUIRE(f.attach() == Status::Ok);
	uint32_t regs[16] = {};
	uint8_t buf[8] = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8};
	bool halted = false;
	CHECK(f.d.read_regs(regs) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.write_reg(5, 0x1234) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.write_reg(0, 0x4600) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.read_mem(0x4400, 4, buf) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.write_mem(0x2400, 4, buf) == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.set_breakpoint(1, 0x4600) == Status::Ok);
	f.t.eem_control_script = {0x0000, 0x0080, 0x0080};
	f.t.captured = {0x0008, 0x0000};
	CHECK(f.d.step() == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.resume() == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.poll(halted) == Status::Ok);
	f.t.eem_control_script = {0x0080, 0x0080};
	f.t.captured = {0x0008, 0x0000};
	CHECK(f.d.halt() == Status::Ok);
	CHECK(f.d.clear_breakpoint(msp430::kNoBreakpoint) == Status::Ok);
	f.d.detach(DetachAction::Run);
	f.running_cpu();
	REQUIRE(f.d.attach(AttachMode::Running, 250000, f.id) == Status::Ok);
	f.d.detach(DetachAction::Reset);

	for (const auto &w : f.t.mem_writes) {
		const bool allowed = w.first == 0x015Cu || w.first == 0x0102u ||
				     (w.first >= 0x2400u && w.first < 0x2408u);
		CHECK(allowed);
		CHECK(!(w.first >= 0x0140u && w.first < 0x0150u)); /* FCTL1..FCTL4 */
	}
	CHECK(f.t.jmb_in.empty()); /* nothing fed to a funclet */
	CHECK(!f.t.jmb_protocol_error);
}
```
