- [ ] **Step 3: Write the server**

Create `tools/msp430/gdbserver.py`:

```python
"""GDB remote-protocol server for an MSP430 behind the probe (sbw.py gdbserver).

Read-only mode (phase B1): registers, memory, run, stop, single step and EEM
hardware breakpoints. Flash is never written: writes outside RAM and the
peripheral registers (the flash controller excluded), software breakpoints
and flash programming are refused here, before anything reaches the probe,
and the probe refuses such writes again (PROTECTED).

One GDB connection at a time. The server attaches when GDB connects and
detaches when GDB detaches, kills, disconnects, or the server is stopped:
the target's program then continues where it stopped.

The register layout is msp430-elf-gdb's built-in one (gdb/msp430-tdep.c):
16 raw registers R0 (PC), R1 (SP), R2 (SR), R3 (CG), R4..R15, 4 bytes each,
little-endian. msp430-tdep.c does not use target descriptions, so none is
sent.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import select
import socket
import struct
import sys

import probe as probe_mod
import rsp
from probe import ProbeError, StatusError

PACKET_SIZE = 0x1000
POLL_INTERVAL = 0.05  # seconds between DBG_STATUS polls while the target runs
ATTACH_TRIES = 4      # normal-entry tries, as flasher.NORMAL_ENTRY_TRIES
ADDR_LIMIT = 0x100000
SIGINT, SIGTRAP = 2, 5

HELP = """monitor commands:
  monitor reset          reset the target and halt at the reset vector
  monitor mode readonly  the only mode of this version: flash is never written
  monitor speed KHZ      SBW clock (100-1000) from the next attach
  monitor status         device, mode, speed, state, hardware breakpoints
"""


class Disconnected(Exception):
    """GDB closed the connection."""


class SessionEnded(Exception):
    """The debug session is over: GDB detached or killed, or the target was lost."""


class GdbServer:
    def __init__(self, probe, device, speed_khz=250, attach_mode=probe_mod.ATTACH_RUNNING,
                 out=sys.stdout, poll_interval=POLL_INTERVAL):
        self.probe = probe
        self.device = device
        self.speed_khz = speed_khz
        self.attach_mode = attach_mode
        self.out = out
        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
        self.mode = "readonly"
        self.attached = False
        self.running = False
        self.slots = []        # hardware breakpoint index -> address or None
        self.regs = None       # cached registers while halted
        self.stop_reply = "S05"
        self._conn = None
        self._reader = None
        self._backlog = []
        self._deferred = []
        self._last = b""

    def say(self, line: str) -> None:
        self.out.write(line + "\n")
        if hasattr(self.out, "flush"):
            self.out.flush()

    # ---- one GDB connection ----

    def serve_connection(self, conn) -> None:
        """Attach, answer GDB's packets until it detaches or goes away, detach.
        A failed attach raises ProbeError; the caller closes the connection."""
        self._conn = conn
        self._reader = rsp.PacketReader()
        self._backlog = []   # events received but not handled yet, in order
        self._deferred = []  # packets that arrived while the target ran
        self._attach(self.attach_mode)
        try:
            while True:
                self._handle_event(*self._next_event(None, deferred=True))
        except (Disconnected, SessionEnded):
            pass
        finally:
            self._detach()

    def _handle_event(self, kind, payload):
        if kind in ("packet", "deferred"):
            if kind == "packet":
                self._conn.sendall(b"+")
            reply = self._dispatch(payload)
            if reply is not None:
                self._send(reply)
        elif kind == "bad":
            self._conn.sendall(b"-")
        elif kind == "nack" and self._last:
            self._conn.sendall(self._last)
        # an interrupt while halted, and acks, need nothing

    def _next_event(self, timeout, deferred=False):
        """The next event: deferred packets first (if asked for), then what
        was already received, then new data from GDB, waiting up to `timeout`
        seconds (None: for ever). None on timeout."""
        if deferred and self._deferred:
            return ("deferred", self._deferred.pop(0))
        while not self._backlog:
            ready, _, _ = select.select([self._conn], [], [], timeout)
            if not ready:
                return None
            try:
                data = self._conn.recv(4096)
            except OSError:
                data = b""
            if not data:
                raise Disconnected()
            self._backlog += self._reader.feed(data)
        return self._backlog.pop(0)

    def _send(self, payload) -> None:
        if isinstance(payload, str):
            payload = payload.encode("latin-1")
        self._last = rsp.frame(payload)
        try:
            self._conn.sendall(self._last)
        except OSError:
            raise Disconnected() from None

    # ---- attach and detach ----

    def _attach(self, mode) -> None:
        last = None
        for _ in range(ATTACH_TRIES):
            try:
                a = self.probe.dbg_attach(mode, self.speed_khz, *self.device.ram)
                break
            except StatusError as e:
                if e.status == probe_mod.STATUS_LOCKED:
                    raise ProbeError("the device's JTAG lock key is programmed; it cannot be debugged") from None
                if e.status != probe_mod.STATUS_NO_TARGET:
                    raise ProbeError(str(e)) from None
                last = e
        else:
            jtag_id = last.data[0] if last.data else 0
            raise ProbeError(f"no MSP430 found (JTAG ID read as 0x{jtag_id:02X}); check the wiring "
                             "and the target supply, or try a lower --speed")
        if a.identity.device_id != self.device.device_id:
            self.probe.dbg_detach(probe_mod.DETACH_RUN)
            raw = a.identity.device_id
            raise ProbeError(f"unexpected device: ID bytes {raw & 0xFF:02X}h {raw >> 8:02X}h, "
                             f"this server is set up for {self.device.name}")
        self.attached = True
        self.running = False
        self.slots = [None] * a.hw_breakpoints
        self.regs = None
        self.stop_reply = self._stop(SIGTRAP, a.pc)
        how = "after a reset" if mode == probe_mod.ATTACH_RESET else "without reset"
        self.say(f"attached to {self.device.name} {how}, halted at 0x{a.pc:05X}; read-only mode; "
                 f"{a.hw_breakpoints} hardware breakpoints")

    def _detach(self) -> None:
        if not self.attached:
            return
        self.attached = False
        try:
            self.probe.dbg_detach(probe_mod.DETACH_RUN)
            self.say("detached; the program continues where it stopped")
        except ProbeError as e:
            self.say(f"detach failed: {e}")

    def _lost(self, e) -> None:
        self.attached = False
        self.running = False
        self.say(f"target lost ({e}); the debug session has ended")

    # ---- packets ----

    def _dispatch(self, p: bytes):
        s = p.decode("latin-1")
        try:
            return self._dispatch_text(s, p)
        except StatusError as e:
            if e.status == probe_mod.STATUS_TARGET_ERROR:
                self._lost(e)
                self._send("E03")
                raise SessionEnded() from None
            self.say(f"error: {e}")
            return "E01"
        except ProbeError as e:
            self._lost(e)
            raise SessionEnded() from None

    def _dispatch_text(self, s: str, p: bytes):
        if s.startswith("qSupported"):
            return f"PacketSize={PACKET_SIZE:x};qXfer:memory-map:read+;swbreak+;hwbreak+"
        if s == "?":
            return self.stop_reply
        if s == "g":
            return "".join(struct.pack("<I", v).hex() for v in self._regs())
        if s.startswith("G"):
            return self._write_all_regs(s[1:])
        if s.startswith("p"):
            n = int(s[1:], 16)
            return struct.pack("<I", self._regs()[n]).hex() if n < 16 else "E01"
        if s.startswith("P"):
            n, value = s[1:].split("=")
            return self._write_reg(int(n, 16), struct.unpack("<I", bytes.fromhex(value))[0])
        if s.startswith("m"):
            addr, length = (int(x, 16) for x in s[1:].split(","))
            return self._read_mem(addr, length)
        if s.startswith("M"):
            head, data = s[1:].split(":", 1)
            addr, _ = (int(x, 16) for x in head.split(","))
            return self._write_mem(addr, bytes.fromhex(data))
        if s.startswith("X"):
            head, data = p[1:].split(b":", 1)
            addr, _ = (int(x, 16) for x in head.split(b","))
            return self._write_mem(addr, rsp.unescape(data))
        if s.startswith("c"):
            return self._continue(s[1:])
        if s.startswith("s"):
            return self._step(s[1:])
        if s == "vCont?":
            return "vCont;c;C;s;S"
        if s.startswith("vCont;"):
            action = s[6:].split(";")[0].split(":")[0]
            return self._step("") if action[0] in "sS" else self._continue("")
        if s.startswith("Z1,"):
            return self._insert_hw(int(s.split(",")[1], 16))
        if s.startswith("z1,"):
            return self._remove_hw(int(s.split(",")[1], 16))
        if s.startswith("Z0,"):
            return self._refuse("software breakpoints are not allowed in read-only mode; use "
                                "hbreak (breakpoints in flash are hardware breakpoints anyway)")
        if s.startswith("z0,"):
            return "OK"
        if s.startswith("vFlash"):
            return self._refuse("flash programming (load) is not allowed in read-only mode")
        if s == "D" or s.startswith("D;"):
            self._detach()
            self._send("OK")
            raise SessionEnded()
        if s in ("k", "vKill") or s.startswith("vKill;"):
            self._detach()
            if s != "k":
                self._send("OK")
            raise SessionEnded()
        if s.startswith("qRcmd,"):
            return self._monitor(bytes.fromhex(s[6:]).decode("utf-8", "replace"))
        if s.startswith("qXfer:memory-map:read::"):
            offset, length = (int(x, 16) for x in s.split("::")[1].split(","))
            xml = self.memory_map_xml().encode()
            part = xml[offset:offset + length]
            return (b"l" if offset + length >= len(xml) else b"m") + rsp.escape(part)
        if s == "qAttached":
            return "1"
        if s.startswith("H") or s == "qSymbol::":
            return "OK"
        return ""

    def _refuse(self, why: str) -> str:
        self.say(f"refused: {why}")
        return "E01"

    def _stop(self, signal: int, pc: int, hwbreak: bool = False) -> str:
        return (f"T{signal:02x}" + ("hwbreak:;" if hwbreak else "") +
                "00:" + struct.pack("<I", pc).hex() + ";")

    # ---- registers ----

    def _regs(self) -> list:
        if self.regs is None:
            self.regs = self.probe.dbg_read_regs()
        return self.regs

    def _write_reg(self, n: int, value: int) -> str:
        if n > 15 or n == 3 or value >= ADDR_LIMIT:
            return "E01"
        self.probe.dbg_write_reg(n, value)
        self.regs = None
        return "OK"

    def _write_all_regs(self, text: str) -> str:
        data = bytes.fromhex(text)
        if len(data) != 64:
            return "E01"
        current = self._regs()
        for n, value in enumerate(struct.unpack("<16I", data)):
            if n != 3 and value != current[n]:
                reply = self._write_reg(n, value)
                if reply != "OK":
                    return reply
        return "OK"

    # ---- memory ----

    def _read_mem(self, addr: int, length: int) -> str:
        length = min(length, PACKET_SIZE // 2)
        if length == 0:
            return ""
        if addr + length > ADDR_LIMIT or not self.device.debug_readable(addr, length):
            return self._refuse(f"read of 0x{addr:05X}-0x{addr + length - 1:05X}: outside the "
                                f"target's memory map (the BSL area "
                                f"0x{self.device.bsl[0]:04X}-0x{self.device.bsl[1] - 1:04X} is never read)")
        start = addr & ~1
        data = self.probe.read_mem(start, (addr + length - start + 1) // 2)
        return data[addr - start:addr - start + length].hex()

    def _write_mem(self, addr: int, data: bytes) -> str:
        if not data:
            return "OK"
        if not self.device.debug_writable(addr, len(data)):
            return self._refuse(f"write of {len(data)} bytes at 0x{addr:05X}: read-only mode writes "
                                "only RAM and peripheral registers (not the flash controller)")
        start, end = addr & ~1, (addr + len(data) + 1) & ~1
        if start != addr or end != addr + len(data):
            buf = bytearray(self.probe.read_mem(start, (end - start) // 2))
        else:
            buf = bytearray(end - start)
        buf[addr - start:addr - start + len(data)] = data
        self.probe.write_mem(start, bytes(buf))
        return "OK"

    # ---- run control ----

    def _set_pc(self, arg: str) -> None:
        if arg:
            self.probe.dbg_write_reg(0, int(arg, 16))

    def _continue(self, arg: str) -> str:
        self._set_pc(arg)
        self.probe.dbg_resume()
        self.running = True
        self.regs = None
        try:
            while True:
                event = self._next_event(self.poll_interval)
                while event is not None:
                    kind, payload = event
                    if kind == "interrupt":
                        self.probe.dbg_halt()
                        st = self.probe.dbg_status()
                        self.running = False
                        signal = SIGTRAP if st.stop_reason == probe_mod.STOP_BREAKPOINT else SIGINT
                        self.stop_reply = self._stop(signal, st.pc,
                                                     st.stop_reason == probe_mod.STOP_BREAKPOINT)
                        return self.stop_reply
                    if kind == "packet":  # all-stop GDB waits; answer it after the stop
                        self._conn.sendall(b"+")
                        self._deferred.append(payload)
                    elif kind == "bad":
                        self._conn.sendall(b"-")
                    event = self._next_event(0)
                st = self.probe.dbg_status()
                if st.halted:
                    self.running = False
                    self.stop_reply = self._stop(SIGTRAP, st.pc,
                                                 st.stop_reason == probe_mod.STOP_BREAKPOINT)
                    return self.stop_reply
        except StatusError as e:
            if e.status != probe_mod.STATUS_TARGET_ERROR:
                raise
            self._lost(e)
            self._send("O" + f"target lost ({e})\n".encode().hex())
            self._send("W00")
            raise SessionEnded() from None

    def _step(self, arg: str) -> str:
        self._set_pc(arg)
        self.regs = None
        try:
            pc = self.probe.dbg_step()
        except StatusError as e:
            if e.status != probe_mod.STATUS_TIMEOUT:
                raise
            pc = self.probe.dbg_status().pc  # halted anyway
            self.say("the step did not complete; the target was halted")
        self.stop_reply = self._stop(SIGTRAP, pc)
        return self.stop_reply

    # ---- breakpoints ----

    def _insert_hw(self, addr: int) -> str:
        if addr in self.slots:
            return "OK"
        if addr & 1 or addr >= ADDR_LIMIT:
            return "E01"
        if None not in self.slots:
            return self._refuse(f"all {len(self.slots)} hardware breakpoints are in use")
        index = self.slots.index(None)
        self.probe.dbg_bp_set(index, addr)
        self.slots[index] = addr
        return "OK"

    def _remove_hw(self, addr: int) -> str:
        if addr in self.slots:
            index = self.slots.index(addr)
            self.probe.dbg_bp_clear(index)
            self.slots[index] = None
        return "OK"

    # ---- monitor ----

    def _monitor(self, command: str) -> str:
        words = command.split()
        if not words or words == ["help"]:
            text = HELP
        elif words == ["reset"]:
            self._attach(probe_mod.ATTACH_RESET)
            text = (f"target reset, halted at 0x{self._pc_of(self.stop_reply):05X}; "
                    "run 'flushregs' so that GDB reads the registers again\n")
        elif words[0] == "mode" and len(words) == 2 and words[1] in ("readonly", "full"):
            text = ("read-only mode: flash is never written\n" if words[1] == "readonly" else
                    "full mode is not available in this version (phase B2); the session stays read-only\n")
        elif words[0] == "speed" and len(words) == 2:
            try:
                khz = int(words[1])
            except ValueError:
                khz = 0
            if 100 <= khz <= 1000:
                self.speed_khz = khz
                text = f"SBW clock {khz} kHz from the next attach (monitor reset, or the next connection)\n"
            else:
                text = "speed must be 100-1000 kHz\n"
        elif words == ["status"]:
            used = sum(1 for s in self.slots if s is not None)
            state = "running" if self.running else f"halted at 0x{self._pc_of(self.stop_reply):05X}"
            text = (f"{self.device.name}, read-only mode, {self.speed_khz} kHz, {state}, "
                    f"hardware breakpoints {used} of {len(self.slots)} in use\n")
        else:
            text = f"unknown monitor command '{command}'\n" + HELP
        self._send("O" + text.encode().hex())
        return "OK"

    @staticmethod
    def _pc_of(stop_reply: str) -> int:
        regs = stop_reply.split("00:")[-1].rstrip(";")
        return struct.unpack("<I", bytes.fromhex(regs))[0]

    def memory_map_xml(self) -> str:
        lines = ['<?xml version="1.0"?>',
                 '<!DOCTYPE memory-map PUBLIC "+//IDN gnu.org//DTD GDB Memory Map V1.0//EN" '
                 '"http://sourceware.org/gdb/gdb-memory-map.dtd">',
                 "<memory-map>"]
        for start, end, kind in self.device.memory_map():
            lines.append(f'  <memory type="{kind}" start="0x{start:x}" length="0x{end - start:x}"/>')
        lines.append("</memory-map>")
        return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"


def serve(server: GdbServer, port: int, host: str = "127.0.0.1") -> None:
    """Accept GDB connections one at a time, until Ctrl-C (KeyboardInterrupt)."""
    listener = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM)
    listener.setsockopt(socket.SOL_SOCKET, socket.SO_REUSEADDR, 1)
    try:
        listener.bind((host, port))
        listener.listen(1)
        while True:
            server.say(f"waiting for GDB on {host}:{listener.getsockname()[1]} "
                       f"(in GDB: target remote :{listener.getsockname()[1]})")
            conn, _ = listener.accept()
            with conn:
                conn.setsockopt(socket.IPPROTO_TCP, socket.TCP_NODELAY, 1)
                try:
                    server.serve_connection(conn)
                except ProbeError as e:
                    server.say(f"error: {e}")
    finally:
        listener.close()
```

### Task 19: `symelf.py`

A symbol-only ELF for firmware that exists only as a flash image. The real tables of the user's board (`~/msp430-backup/fw-rebuild/notes/names.tsv`: address, old_name, new_name, confidence, purpose; `objnames.tsv`: address, old_name, new_name, kind, confidence, meaning) have no size column, and `objnames.tsv` mixes in type rows (address `-`, kind `struct`/`union`) and struct members (kind `member`, whose names repeat, such as `crc` or `unit`); the tool skips those. The `.MSP430.attributes` section is what makes `msp430-elf-gdb` choose MSP430X with 20-bit addresses ("Read first"). The tables and images stay outside the repository; the tests use small made-up tables.

**Files:**
- Create: `tools/msp430/tests/test_symelf.py`
- Create: `tools/msp430/symelf.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tools/msp430/tests/test_symelf.py`:

```python
import io
import os
import struct
import sys
import tempfile
import unittest

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))

import devices  # noqa: E402
import image  # noqa: E402
import symelf  # noqa: E402
from image import Segment  # noqa: E402

NAMES = ("address\told_name\tnew_name\tconfidence\tpurpose\n"
         "05472\tdemo_old_05472\tdemo_func_05472\thigh\tdemo\n"
         "1450E\tdemo_old_1450E\tdemo_far_func_1450E\thigh\tdemo\n")
OBJECTS = ("address\told_name\tnew_name\tkind\tconfidence\tmeaning\n"
           "2404\tdemo_old_2404\tdemo_var_2404\tvar\tmedium\tdemo\n"
           "-\tdemo_old_type\tt_demo\tstruct\thigh\tdemo\n"
           "258E\tt_demo.m258E\tdemo_member\tmember\thigh\tdemo\n"
           "0200\tdemo_old_0200\tdemo_periph_0200\tvar\thigh\tdemo\n"
           "4640\tdemo_old_4640\tdemo_const\tconst\thigh\tdemo\n")


def read_elf(data):
    """A small readelf: header fields, sections by name, symbols."""
    assert data[:4] == b"\x7fELF" and data[4] == 1 and data[5] == 1
    (e_type, e_machine, _, e_entry, _, e_shoff, e_flags, _, _, e_phnum, e_shentsize, e_shnum,
     e_shstrndx) = struct.unpack_from("<HHIIIIIHHHHHH", data, 16)
    shdrs = [struct.unpack_from("<10I", data, e_shoff + i * e_shentsize) for i in range(e_shnum)]
    shstr = shdrs[e_shstrndx]
    names = data[shstr[4]:shstr[4] + shstr[5]]

    def name_at(table, off):
        return table[off:table.index(b"\0", off)].decode()

    sections = {name_at(names, s[0]): s for s in shdrs[1:]}
    sym, strt = sections[".symtab"], sections[".strtab"]
    strings = data[strt[4]:strt[4] + strt[5]]
    symbols = {}
    for off in range(sym[4] + 16, sym[4] + sym[5], 16):
        st_name, value, size, info, _, shndx = struct.unpack_from("<IIIBBH", data, off)
        symbols[name_at(strings, st_name)] = (value, size, info & 0xF, info >> 4, shndx)
    return {"type": e_type, "machine": e_machine, "entry": e_entry, "flags": e_flags,
            "phnum": e_phnum, "sections": sections, "symbols": symbols,
            "index": {name_at(names, s[0]): i for i, s in enumerate(shdrs) if i}}


class SymElfTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        self.tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
        self.names = self.path("names.tsv", NAMES)
        self.objects = self.path("objnames.tsv", OBJECTS)

    def tearDown(self):
        self.tmp.cleanup()

    def path(self, name, text=None):
        p = os.path.join(self.tmp.name, name)
        if text is not None:
            with open(p, "w") as f:
                f.write(text)
        return p

    def run_tool(self, *args):
        out, err = io.StringIO(), io.StringIO()
        code = symelf.main(list(args), out=out, err=err)
        return code, out.getvalue(), err.getvalue()

    def test_symbols_and_sections(self):
        elf_path = self.path("sym.elf")
        code, out, _ = self.run_tool(self.names, self.objects, "-o", elf_path)
        self.assertEqual(code, 0)
        self.assertIn("2 functions, 3 objects", out)
        with open(elf_path, "rb") as f:
            elf = read_elf(f.read())
        self.assertEqual((elf["type"], elf["machine"], elf["flags"], elf["phnum"]), (2, 105, 45, 0))
        text = elf["sections"][".text"]
        self.assertEqual((text[1], text[2], text[3], text[5]), (8, 6, 0x4400, 0x20000))  # NOBITS
        bss = elf["sections"][".bss"]
        self.assertEqual((bss[1], bss[3], bss[5]), (8, 0x1C00, 0x2800))
        syms = elf["symbols"]
        self.assertEqual(syms["demo_func_05472"], (0x5472, 0, 2, 1, elf["index"][".text"]))
        self.assertEqual(syms["demo_far_func_1450E"][0], 0x1450E)
        self.assertEqual(syms["demo_var_2404"], (0x2404, 0, 1, 1, elf["index"][".bss"]))
        self.assertEqual(syms["demo_periph_0200"][4], 0xFFF1)  # absolute
        self.assertEqual(syms["demo_const"][2:], (1, 1, elf["index"][".text"]))
        self.assertNotIn("t_demo", syms)
        self.assertNotIn("demo_member", syms)

    def test_attributes_select_msp430x_large(self):
        attr = symelf.attributes()
        self.assertEqual(attr[0:1], b"A")
        length = struct.unpack_from("<I", attr, 1)[0]
        self.assertEqual(length, len(attr) - 1)
        self.assertEqual(attr[5:12], b"mspabi\0")
        self.assertEqual(attr[12], 1)  # Tag_File
        self.assertEqual(struct.unpack_from("<I", attr, 13)[0], len(attr) - 12)
        self.assertEqual(attr[17:], bytes([4, 2, 6, 2, 8, 2]))
        elf_path = self.path("sym.elf")
        self.run_tool(self.names, "-o", elf_path)
        with open(elf_path, "rb") as f:
            data = f.read()
        sec = read_elf(data)["sections"][".MSP430.attributes"]
        self.assertEqual(sec[1], 0x70000003)
        self.assertEqual(data[sec[4]:sec[4] + sec[5]], attr)

    def test_image_fills_text(self):
        hex_path = self.path("fw.hex")
        image.save(hex_path, [Segment(0x4400, b"\x31\x40\x00\x44"), Segment(0xFFFE, b"\x1A\x44")])
        elf_path = self.path("sym.elf")
        code, out, _ = self.run_tool(self.names, "--image", hex_path, "-o", elf_path)
        self.assertEqual(code, 0)
        self.assertIn("main flash from the image", out)
        with open(elf_path, "rb") as f:
            data = f.read()
        elf = read_elf(data)
        text = elf["sections"][".text"]
        self.assertEqual(text[1], 1)  # PROGBITS
        body = data[text[4]:text[4] + text[5]]
        self.assertEqual(body[:4], b"\x31\x40\x00\x44")
        self.assertEqual(body[4], 0xFF)
        self.assertEqual(body[0xFFFE - 0x4400:0x10000 - 0x4400], b"\x1A\x44")
        self.assertEqual(elf["entry"], 0x441A)

    def test_errors(self):
        out = self.path("x.elf")
        bad = self.path("bad.tsv", "address\tnew_name\tkind\n4400\tf\tbogus\n")
        self.assertIn("unknown kind 'bogus'", self.run_tool(bad, "-o", out)[2])
        dup = self.path("dup.tsv", "address\tnew_name\n4400\tf\n4500\tf\n")
        self.assertIn("duplicate symbol 'f'", self.run_tool(dup, "-o", out)[2])
        noaddr = self.path("noaddr.tsv", "name\nf\n")
        self.assertIn("no 'address' column", self.run_tool(noaddr, "-o", out)[2])
        far = self.path("far.tsv", "address\tname\n100000\tf\n")
        self.assertIn("outside the 20-bit space", self.run_tool(far, "-o", out)[2])
        self.assertFalse(os.path.exists(out))

    def test_refuses_to_overwrite_without_force(self):
        out = self.path("x.elf", "keep")
        code, _, err = self.run_tool(self.names, "-o", out)
        self.assertEqual(code, 1)
        self.assertIn("already exists", err)
        self.assertEqual(self.run_tool(self.names, "-o", out, "--force")[0], 0)

    def test_same_symbol_twice_is_kept_once(self):
        out = self.path("x.elf")
        self.assertEqual(self.run_tool(self.names, self.names, "-o", out)[0], 0)
        with open(out, "rb") as f:
            self.assertEqual(len(read_elf(f.read())["symbols"]), 2)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the tool**

Create `tools/msp430/symelf.py` and make it executable (`chmod +x tools/msp430/symelf.py`):

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Write a symbol-only ELF file for msp430-elf-gdb from name tables.

    symelf.py names.tsv [objnames.tsv ...] -o symbols.elf [--image backup.hex]

Each table is tab-separated with a header row. Columns used:
  address   hex, with or without 0x; "-" (a type, not a symbol) skips the row
  new_name  the symbol name (or a column called "name")
  kind      optional: func (the default when the column is missing), var,
            const, data; struct, union and member rows are skipped
  size      optional, decimal or 0x hex; 0 when missing

The file is an ELF32 for the MSP430X (EM_MSP430) with the attributes
msp430-elf-gdb reads to choose the MSP430X instruction set and the large code
model (a .MSP430.attributes section). Main flash is the .text section and RAM
the .bss section, both without contents; with --image, .text holds the
image's main-flash bytes, so GDB can disassemble without a target. Symbols in
neither are absolute. Tables and images of third-party firmware stay outside
the repository.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import os
import struct
import sys

import devices
import image

EM_MSP430 = 105
E_MSP430_MACH_MSP430X = 45
SHT_PROGBITS, SHT_SYMTAB, SHT_STRTAB, SHT_NOBITS = 1, 2, 3, 8
SHT_MSP430_ATTRIBUTES = 0x70000003
SHF_WRITE, SHF_ALLOC, SHF_EXECINSTR = 1, 2, 4
STB_GLOBAL, STT_OBJECT, STT_FUNC = 1, 1, 2
SHN_ABS = 0xFFF1
# OFBA_MSPABI tags and values (binutils include/elf/msp430.h)
TAG_ISA, TAG_CODE_MODEL, TAG_DATA_MODEL = 4, 6, 8
ISA_MSP430X, MODEL_LARGE = 2, 2

FUNC_KINDS = {"func", "function", "code"}
OBJECT_KINDS = {"var", "const", "data", "object"}
SKIP_KINDS = {"struct", "union", "member", "type"}


class SymbolError(Exception):
    """A table cannot be turned into symbols; the message names the row."""


def read_table(path: str) -> list:
    """[(name, address, size, STT_FUNC | STT_OBJECT)] from one table."""
    with open(path, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        rows = list(csv.reader(f, delimiter="\t"))
    if not rows:
        raise SymbolError(f"{path}: empty table")
    header = [h.strip() for h in rows[0]]
    if "address" not in header:
        raise SymbolError(f"{path}: no 'address' column")
    name_col = "new_name" if "new_name" in header else "name"
    if name_col not in header:
        raise SymbolError(f"{path}: no 'new_name' or 'name' column")
    col = {h: i for i, h in enumerate(header)}
    symbols = []
    for line, row in enumerate(rows[1:], start=2):
        if not row or not any(cell.strip() for cell in row):
            continue

        def cell(name, default=""):
            i = col.get(name)
            return row[i].strip() if i is not None and i < len(row) else default

        addr_text = cell("address")
        kind = cell("kind", "func").lower() or "func"
        if addr_text in ("", "-") or kind in SKIP_KINDS:
            continue
        if kind in FUNC_KINDS:
            stype = STT_FUNC
        elif kind in OBJECT_KINDS:
            stype = STT_OBJECT
        else:
            raise SymbolError(f"{path}:{line}: unknown kind '{kind}'")
        name = cell(name_col)
        if not name:
            raise SymbolError(f"{path}:{line}: no name")
        try:
            addr = int(addr_text, 16)
            size = int(cell("size", "0") or "0", 0)
        except ValueError:
            raise SymbolError(f"{path}:{line}: bad address or size") from None
        if not 0 <= addr < 0x100000:
            raise SymbolError(f"{path}:{line}: address 0x{addr:X} outside the 20-bit space")
        symbols.append((name, addr, size, stype))
    return symbols


def _uleb(value: int) -> bytes:
    out = bytearray()
    while True:
        byte = value & 0x7F
        value >>= 7
        out.append(byte | (0x80 if value else 0))
        if not value:
            return bytes(out)


def attributes() -> bytes:
    """.MSP430.attributes: format 'A', vendor "mspabi", file-scope attributes
    ISA = MSP430X, code model = large, data model = large."""
    attrs = (_uleb(TAG_ISA) + _uleb(ISA_MSP430X) + _uleb(TAG_CODE_MODEL) + _uleb(MODEL_LARGE) +
             _uleb(TAG_DATA_MODEL) + _uleb(MODEL_LARGE))
    file_sub = b"\x01" + struct.pack("<I", 5 + len(attrs)) + attrs
    vendor = b"mspabi\0"
    return b"A" + struct.pack("<I", 4 + len(vendor) + len(file_sub)) + vendor + file_sub


def build(symbols: list, device, text_data: bytes | None = None, entry: int = 0) -> bytes:
    """The ELF file as bytes. text_data: main-flash contents, or None."""
    seen = {}
    for name, addr, _, _ in symbols:
        if name in seen and seen[name] != addr:
            raise SymbolError(f"duplicate symbol '{name}' at 0x{seen[name]:05X} and 0x{addr:05X}")
        seen[name] = addr
    symbols = sorted({(n, a, s, t) for n, a, s, t in symbols}, key=lambda x: (x[1], x[0]))

    text_lo, text_hi = device.main_start, device.main_end
    bss_lo, bss_hi = device.ram
    shstr = b"\0.text\0.bss\0.MSP430.attributes\0.symtab\0.strtab\0.shstrtab\0"

    def sh_name(n):
        return shstr.index(n.encode() + b"\0")

    strtab = bytearray(b"\0")
    symtab = bytearray(16)  # symbol 0
    for name, addr, size, stype in symbols:
        if text_lo <= addr < text_hi:
            shndx = 1
        elif bss_lo <= addr < bss_hi:
            shndx = 2
        else:
            shndx = SHN_ABS
        symtab += struct.pack("<IIIBBH", len(strtab), addr, size, (STB_GLOBAL << 4) | stype, 0, shndx)
        strtab += name.encode() + b"\0"

    attr = attributes()
    body = bytearray()
    offsets = {}

    def place(key, data):
        offsets[key] = 52 + len(body)
        body.extend(data)
        while len(body) % 4:
            body.append(0)

    if text_data is not None:
        place("text", text_data)
    place("attr", attr)
    place("symtab", symtab)
    place("strtab", strtab)
    place("shstr", shstr)
    shoff = 52 + len(body)

    def shdr(name, stype, flags, addr, off, size, link=0, info=0, align=1, entsize=0):
        return struct.pack("<10I", name, stype, flags, addr, off, size, link, info, align, entsize)

    sections = [
        bytes(40),
        shdr(sh_name(".text"), SHT_PROGBITS if text_data is not None else SHT_NOBITS,
             SHF_ALLOC | SHF_EXECINSTR, text_lo, offsets.get("text", shoff), text_hi - text_lo,
             align=2),
        shdr(sh_name(".bss"), SHT_NOBITS, SHF_ALLOC | SHF_WRITE, bss_lo, shoff, bss_hi - bss_lo, align=2),
        shdr(sh_name(".MSP430.attributes"), SHT_MSP430_ATTRIBUTES, 0, 0, offsets["attr"], len(attr)),
        shdr(sh_name(".symtab"), SHT_SYMTAB, 0, 0, offsets["symtab"], len(symtab), link=5, info=1,
             align=4, entsize=16),
        shdr(sh_name(".strtab"), SHT_STRTAB, 0, 0, offsets["strtab"], len(strtab)),
        shdr(sh_name(".shstrtab"), SHT_STRTAB, 0, 0, offsets["shstr"], len(shstr)),
    ]
    ident = b"\x7fELF" + bytes([1, 1, 1, 0]) + bytes(8)  # ELF32, little-endian, version 1
    header = ident + struct.pack("<HHIIIIIHHHHHH", 2, EM_MSP430, 1, entry, 0, shoff,
                                 E_MSP430_MACH_MSP430X, 52, 0, 0, 40, len(sections), 6)
    return header + bytes(body) + b"".join(sections)


def main(argv=None, out=sys.stdout, err=sys.stderr) -> int:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="symelf.py", description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    p.add_argument("tables", nargs="+", help="tab-separated name tables")
    p.add_argument("-o", "--out", required=True, help="the ELF file to write")
    p.add_argument("--image", help="Intel HEX or TI-TXT image: main flash goes into .text")
    p.add_argument("--force", action="store_true", help="replace the output file if it exists")
    args = p.parse_args(argv)
    device = devices.MSP430F5529
    try:
        if os.path.exists(args.out) and not args.force:
            raise SymbolError(f"{args.out} already exists; choose another name or pass --force")
        symbols = []
        for path in args.tables:
            symbols += read_table(path)
        symbols = list(dict.fromkeys(symbols))  # the same row in two tables counts once
        text, entry = None, 0
        if args.image:
            flash = bytearray(b"\xFF" * (device.main_end - device.main_start))
            for seg in image.load(args.image):
                for i, b in enumerate(seg.data):
                    if device.main_start <= seg.addr + i < device.main_end:
                        flash[seg.addr + i - device.main_start] = b
            text = bytes(flash)
            entry = flash[0xFFFE - device.main_start] | (flash[0xFFFF - device.main_start] << 8)
        data = build(symbols, device, text, entry)
        with open(args.out, "wb") as f:
            f.write(data)
    except (SymbolError, image.ImageError, OSError) as e:
        err.write(f"error: {e}\n")
        return 1
    functions = sum(1 for s in symbols if s[3] == STT_FUNC)
    out.write(f"wrote {args.out}: {functions} functions, {len(symbols) - functions} objects"
              + (", main flash from the image" if text is not None else "") + "\n")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

### Task 20: `sbw.py gdbserver`

`sbw.py gdbserver [--port 3333] [--attach | --reset] [--speed KHZ] [--allow-flash-write]`. It must not go through Phase A's `open_session` (its `CONNECT` resets the target): it checks the probe (`DAP_Disconnect`, `INFO` version 2 with the debug feature), configures the chunk sizes and hands over to `gdbserver.serve`. `--allow-flash-write` is B2 and refused here. `sbw.py info` also prints the probe's protocol version and debug support.

```python
class GdbServerCliTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def run_server(self, argv, fw):
        calls = []

        def fake_serve(server, port):
            calls.append((server, port))

        out, err = io.StringIO(), io.StringIO()
        code = sbw.main(argv, transport=fw, out=out, err=err, serve=fake_serve)
        return code, out.getvalue(), err.getvalue(), calls

    def test_defaults_attach_without_reset(self):
        fw = FakeFirmware()
        code, _, _, calls = self.run_server(["gdbserver"], fw)
        self.assertEqual(code, 0)
        server, port = calls[0]
        self.assertEqual((port, server.speed_khz, server.attach_mode), (3333, 250, 0))
        self.assertEqual(fw.connects, [])  # no CONNECT: it would reset the target
        self.assertEqual(fw.ops, [("dap_disconnect",)])
        self.assertTrue(fw.closed)

    def test_options(self):
        fw = FakeFirmware()
        _, _, _, calls = self.run_server(["gdbserver", "--port", "4444", "--reset", "--speed", "500"], fw)
        server, port = calls[0]
        self.assertEqual((port, server.speed_khz, server.attach_mode), (4444, 500, 1))
        _, _, _, calls = self.run_server(["--speed", "100", "gdbserver"], FakeFirmware())
        self.assertEqual(calls[0][0].speed_khz, 100)

    def test_attach_and_reset_exclude_each_other(self):
        with contextlib.redirect_stderr(io.StringIO()):
            with self.assertRaises(SystemExit):
                sbw.main(["gdbserver", "--attach", "--reset"], transport=FakeFirmware())

    def test_allow_flash_write_is_refused(self):
        fw = FakeFirmware()
        code, _, err, calls = self.run_server(["gdbserver", "--allow-flash-write"], fw)
        self.assertEqual((code, calls), (1, []))
        self.assertIn("phase B2", err)
        self.assertEqual(fw.ops, [])

    def test_old_probe_firmware_is_refused(self):
        code, _, err, calls = self.run_server(["gdbserver"], FakeFirmware(version=1, features=1))
        self.assertEqual((code, calls), (1, []))
        self.assertIn("does not support debugging", err)

    def test_info_reports_debugging(self):
        code, out, _ = run(["info"], FakeFirmware())
        self.assertEqual(code, 0)
        self.assertIn("probe: SBW protocol version 2, debugging supported, 8 hardware breakpoints", out)
        code, out, _ = run(["info"], FakeFirmware(version=1, features=1))
        self.assertIn("probe: SBW protocol version 1, no debugging support", out)
```

```python
def _gdbserver(args, probe: SbwProbe, out, serve) -> None:
    """Check the probe, then serve GDB connections until Ctrl-C. No CONNECT:
    a Phase A session would reset the target."""
    if args.allow_flash_write:
        raise FlashError("--allow-flash-write (full mode) comes with phase B2; this version is "
                         "read-only and never writes flash")
    speed = args.gdb_speed or args.speed
    probe.dap_disconnect()
    info = probe.info()
    if info.version < 2 or not info.features & probe_mod.FEATURE_DEBUG:
        raise FlashError("the probe firmware does not support debugging (SBW protocol version "
                         f"{info.version}); flash a firmware built from this version")
    probe.configure(speed)
    mode = probe_mod.ATTACH_RESET if args.reset else probe_mod.ATTACH_RUNNING
    server = gdbserver.GdbServer(probe, devices.MSP430F5529, speed_khz=speed,
                                 attach_mode=mode, out=out)
    serve(server, args.port)


def main(argv=None, transport=None, out=sys.stdout, err=sys.stderr, serve=gdbserver.serve) -> int:
    args = _parser().parse_args(argv)
    if args.cmd == "gdbserver":
        try:
            t = transport if transport is not None else probe_mod.UsbTransport(args.serial)
            try:
                _gdbserver(args, SbwProbe(t), out, serve)
            finally:
                try:
                    t.close()
                except (ProbeError, OSError):
                    pass
        except (ProbeError, FlashError, OSError) as e:
            err.write(f"error: {e}\n")
            return 1
        except KeyboardInterrupt:
            err.write("interrupted; the GDB server has stopped\n")
            return 130
        return 0
    try:
        segments = _prepare(args)
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the user guide**

Create `docs/MSP430_DEBUG.md`:

````markdown
# Debugging an MSP430F5529 with GDB (read-only mode)

The probe can debug an MSP430F5529 over Spy-Bi-Wire: halt, resume,
single-step, CPU registers, memory, and up to eight hardware breakpoints.
`sbw.py gdbserver` speaks GDB's remote protocol and drives the probe; GDB is
`msp430-elf-gdb` from TI's MSP430-GCC package. Wiring and electrical checks
are those of `docs/MSP430_SBW.md`.

This version has one mode, **read-only**. It is meant for boards whose
firmware must not change.

## What read-only means

Flash (main flash, information memory, bootloader) is never written.
Three layers refuse it independently:

1. GDB is told that flash is read-only memory (the memory map marks it
   `rom`), so it does not try to write there and uses hardware breakpoints
   for code in flash.
2. The server refuses every write outside RAM and the peripheral registers,
   software breakpoints and `load`, before anything is sent to the probe.
3. The probe firmware refuses such writes again (status `PROTECTED`) and
   refuses the erase and flash commands while a debug session is open.

What can be written: RAM (0x1C00-0x43FF, USB RAM included) and peripheral
registers (0x0000-0x0FFF) except the flash controller (0x0140-0x014F). The
bootloader area (0x1000-0x17FF) is neither read nor written.

## Starting

```bash
python3 tools/msp430/sbw.py gdbserver            # attach to the running program
python3 tools/msp430/sbw.py gdbserver --reset    # reset, halt at the reset vector
```

Options: `--port PORT` (default 3333, on 127.0.0.1 only), `--speed KHZ`
(default 250). The server waits for GDB; it attaches when GDB connects and
detaches when GDB leaves. Stop it with Ctrl-C.

In a second terminal:

```
msp430-elf-gdb -nx symbols.elf
(gdb) set remote hardware-breakpoint-limit 8
(gdb) target remote :3333
```

Attaching without reset stops the program where it is; the target's other
state (watchdog, low-power mode, peripherals) is kept as TI's debug stack
keeps it. When GDB detaches (`detach`, `quit`, `kill`, or the connection
drops) the program continues where it stopped, without a reset.

## Symbols for firmware without an ELF file

GDB needs an ELF file to know the MSP430X architecture and the symbol names.
For firmware that exists only as a flash image, `symelf.py` makes a
symbol-only ELF from name tables:

```bash
python3 tools/msp430/symelf.py names.tsv objnames.tsv --image backup.hex -o symbols.elf
```

The tables are tab-separated with a header row: `address` (hex), `new_name`
or `name`, optionally `kind` (`func` by default; `var`, `const`, `data`;
`struct`, `union` and `member` rows are skipped) and `size`. With `--image`
the ELF also contains main flash, so `disassemble` works without a target.
Keep tables and images of third-party firmware outside the repository.

## Using GDB

| Task | GDB |
|---|---|
| Registers | `info registers`, `p $r12`, `set var $r12 = 5` |
| Memory | `x/16xh 0x2400`, `p some_variable` |
| Stop a running program | Ctrl-C |
| Continue | `continue` |
| One instruction | `stepi`, `nexti` |
| Breakpoint | `break func` or `hbreak *0x4600` |
| Leave, program keeps running | `detach` |

- **Breakpoints** are the chip's eight EEM triggers. In flash every `break`
  is a hardware breakpoint. GDB's `next`, `finish` and `until` need one
  trigger each for their temporary breakpoints; with all eight in use they
  fail with "Could not insert hardware breakpoint".
- **Software breakpoints** (a `break` on an address in RAM) are refused in
  read-only mode; use `hbreak` there.
- **Source-level stepping** needs debug information that matches the flash;
  for firmware without it, step by instruction.
- **Reading peripheral registers can have side effects** (interrupt vector
  registers clear flags, receive buffers clear their flag). Read only the
  registers you need.
- **Byte writes** to RAM or peripherals are word read-modify-writes.

## Monitor commands

| Command | Effect |
|---|---|
| `monitor reset` | reset the target and halt at the reset vector; then `flushregs` |
| `monitor status` | device, mode, speed, state, breakpoints in use |
| `monitor speed KHZ` | SBW clock for the next attach |
| `monitor mode readonly` | the only mode of this version |

## While the CPU is halted

- The watchdog is held and gets its own setting back on resume and detach.
- MCLK, SMCLK and ACLK stop (EEM clock control 0x000E, the value TI's debug
  stack writes when it takes the CPU). Timers clocked from them stop too;
  see the acceptance record in `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`, Phase 7, for what
  was measured on the F5529 board.
- A CPU in a low-power mode is taken over without waking it; on resume it
  goes back to the same mode.

## Troubleshooting

| Message | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `refused: write of ... read-only mode writes only RAM and peripheral registers` | The address is flash, the bootloader, the flash controller or vacant memory. |
| `refused: software breakpoints are not allowed in read-only mode` | Use `hbreak`. |
| `refused: all 8 hardware breakpoints are in use` | Delete a breakpoint. |
| `refused: read of ... outside the target's memory map` | The bootloader area and vacant memory are not read. |
| `target lost (...)` | The target was reset, lost power or the wires came off. The session has ended; start GDB's `target remote` again. |
| `the probe firmware does not support debugging` | Flash the probe with a firmware built from this version. |
| `no MSP430 found` | See `docs/MSP430_SBW.md`, Troubleshooting. |

Do not run SWD tools against the probe while an MSP430 is attached: they
drive the same two pins.

## How it works

`lib/msp430/src/debug.cpp` ports the CPUXv2 debug sequences of TI's MSP
Debug Stack (MSPDS-OPEN-SOURCE 3.15.1.1): taking a running CPU under JTAG
control and saving its context, reset and halt, register and memory access
that keep the context, single step, release with EEM breakpoints armed, and
stop detection. They are exposed as operations 0x09-0x12 of the CMSIS-DAP
vendor command 0x90. `tools/msp430/gdbserver.py` translates GDB's remote
protocol into those operations. The design is
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-msp430-debug-design.md`; TI's licence
notice is in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.
````

```bash
S=$(mktemp -d)
```

- a table whose first 16 data rows are the raw registers: name `''`, `Nr` and `Rel` 0–15, `Offset` 0, 4, …, 60, `Size` 4, `Type` `uint32_t`, `Rmt Nr` 0–15, `g/G Offset` 0, 4, …, 60; then the 16 pseudo-registers `pc`, `sp`, `sr`, `cg`, `r4`…`r15` without remote numbers.

If any of this differs, **stop and report** the output: the server's `g`/`p` code or `symelf.py`'s attributes must change first, and nothing in Tasks 23 and 25 may run.

### Task 23: End-to-end test with `msp430-elf-gdb --batch`

A real GDB against the server and the fake firmware: connect, registers, a breakpoint by symbol (auto hardware, `Z1`), continue to it, `stepi`, memory read and a RAM write, detach; and the read-only refusals (a write into flash, `load`, a software breakpoint in RAM). The test is skipped where `msp430-elf-gdb` is not on `PATH`, so CI and machines without GDB still pass.

**Files:**

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the test**

Create `tools/msp430/tests/test_gdb_e2e.py`:

```python
"""End to end: msp430-elf-gdb --batch against the GDB server and the fake
firmware. Skipped unless msp430-elf-gdb is on PATH (TI's MSP430-GCC)."""
import io
import os
import shutil
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import threading
import unittest

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))

import devices  # noqa: E402
import gdbserver  # noqa: E402
import image  # noqa: E402
import symelf  # noqa: E402
from fake_firmware import FakeFirmware  # noqa: E402
from probe import SbwProbe  # noqa: E402

GDB = shutil.which("msp430-elf-gdb")


@unittest.skipUnless(GDB, "msp430-elf-gdb is not installed")
class GdbEndToEndTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        self.tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
        names = os.path.join(self.tmp.name, "names.tsv")
        with open(names, "w") as f:
            f.write("address\tnew_name\n4520\tmain_loop\n4600\tkey_handler\n")
        self.elf = os.path.join(self.tmp.name, "symbols.elf")
        self.assertEqual(symelf.main([names, "-o", self.elf], out=io.StringIO()), 0)
        hex_path = os.path.join(self.tmp.name, "fw.hex")
        image.save(hex_path, [image.Segment(0x4400, b"\x31\x40\x00\x44")])
        self.image_elf = os.path.join(self.tmp.name, "image.elf")  # .text with contents: loadable
        self.assertEqual(symelf.main([names, "--image", hex_path, "-o", self.image_elf],
                                     out=io.StringIO()), 0)
        self.fw = FakeFirmware()
        self.fw.mem.update({0x4600: 0x30, 0x4601: 0x41})  # RET at key_handler
        self.out = io.StringIO()
        self.server = gdbserver.GdbServer(SbwProbe(self.fw), devices.MSP430F5529, out=self.out,
                                          poll_interval=0.001)
        self.listener = socket.socket()
        self.listener.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
        self.listener.listen(1)
        self.port = self.listener.getsockname()[1]
        self.thread = threading.Thread(target=self._serve, daemon=True)
        self.thread.start()

    def _serve(self):
        conn, _ = self.listener.accept()
        with conn:
            self.server.serve_connection(conn)

    def tearDown(self):
        self.listener.close()
        self.thread.join(5)
        self.tmp.cleanup()

    def gdb(self, *commands, elf=None):
        argv = [GDB, "--batch", "-nx", "-ex", "set confirm off",
                "-ex", f"target remote 127.0.0.1:{self.port}"]
        for c in commands:
            argv += ["-ex", c]
        argv.append(elf or self.elf)
        done = subprocess.run(argv, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=60)
        return done.stdout + done.stderr

    def test_session(self):
        text = self.gdb("maint print remote-registers", "info registers pc sp r12",
                        "break key_handler", "continue", "stepi", "x/2xb 0x2400",
                        "set var *(unsigned char *)0x2400 = 0x5a", "detach")
        self.assertIn("key_handler", text)
        self.assertRegex(text, r"pc\s+0x4520")
        self.assertIn("Breakpoint 1, 0x00004600 in key_handler", text)
        self.assertEqual(self.fw.mem.get(0x2400), 0x5A)
        self.assertEqual(self.fw.detaches, [0])
        # 16 raw registers, 4 bytes each, remote numbers 0..15 at g/G offset 4n
        # (maint print remote-registers: Name Nr Rel Offset Size Type Rmt-Nr g/G-Offset)
        for n in range(16):
            self.assertRegex(text, rf"''\s+{n}\s+{n}\s+{4 * n}\s+4\s+uint32_t\s+{n}\s+{4 * n}\b")

    def test_read_only_refusals(self):
        text = self.gdb("set var *(unsigned short *)0x4400 = 0", "load", "break *0x2400",
                        "continue", "detach", elf=self.image_elf)
        self.assertIn("Cannot access memory at address 0x4400", text)
        self.assertRegex(text, r"Load failed|Cannot access memory|Writing to flash memory forbidden")
        self.assertRegex(text, r"Cannot insert breakpoint")  # Z0 in RAM refused
        self.assertEqual(self.fw.write_chunks, [])
        self.assertEqual(self.fw.mem.get(0x4400), None)
        self.assertEqual(self.fw.resumes, 0)  # GDB does not resume with a breakpoint missing
        self.assertEqual(self.fw.detaches, [0])
```

These are the only expectations in this plan that were written without the real GDB at hand. If a test fails:

- because GDB words a message differently while the behaviour is right (the breakpoint was hit, nothing was written to flash, the layout matches), change only the expected text to GDB's actual wording, and say so in the commit message;
- because of behaviour (a write reached the fake, the target was resumed with a breakpoint missing, the register table differs): fix the server, never the expectation, and re-run all tests.

- [ ] **Step 4: Run all Python tests**

Run: `PATH="$HOME/Developer/msp430-gcc/bin:$PATH" python3 -m unittest discover -s tools/msp430/tests`
Expected: `Ran 221 tests` and `OK` (with GDB), or `OK (skipped=2)` (without).

### Task 25: On-target acceptance B1 (manual, on the user's board)

The spec's "On-target acceptance, B1", on the user's MSP430F5529 board with the rpi_pico probe soldered to it. The user must be at the board: to put the probe into BOOTSEL, to watch the display and to press keys. Ask the user before starting, and run nothing on the board without them.

**Safety rules (from the user; follow them literally):**

- The board's firmware exists nowhere else except the backups in `~/msp430-backup/` ("Flash要先备份，千万不要破坏", "要非常小心").
- B1 never writes flash.
- Before the first on-target step and after the last, read back the full main flash and the information memory and compare them with the backups (`~/msp430-backup/main-250.hex`, `info.hex`).
- Do not read the BSL area 0x1000–0x17FF on this board (reading it reset the CPU in Phase A).
- Never drive SWD while the MSP430 is attached (the probe's GP2/GP3 are soldered to the board): no openocd, probe-rs, `tools/conformance` or any other SWD tool.
- Stop at the first unexpected result and report instead of retrying with variations.

In addition, for this session:

- No memory writes on this board, not even to RAM; no `flash`, `erase`, `trial` or `selftest`.
- The firmware may store data in flash on its own (the local acceptance notes say what makes it do so). So during the session the user does nothing that could make it store data (the local acceptance notes list the keys and actions to avoid); navigating between screens is fine. Ask the user which keys are safe to press before Step 6. A difference in the final compare can come from the firmware itself; report it with its addresses and do not "repair" anything.
- Every `sbw.py` command except `gdbserver` resets the target (as in Phase 6); that is expected.

Preconditions: Tasks 1–24 done, all tests green, Task 22 found the expected register layout and Task 23 passed with the real GDB (the refusals of Step 8 were then already proven against the fake). If Task 23 did not run, stop after Step 4 and report.

**Files:**
- Modify: `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: Flash the probe**

The user holds BOOTSEL while plugging in the Pico (the MSP430 board stays connected as it is). Copy `build/rpi_pico/zephyr/zephyr.uf2` (from Task 24's build) onto the `RPI-RP2` drive. Nothing else.

- [ ] **Step 2: Acceptance 0: flash and information memory equal the backups**

```bash
mkdir -p ~/msp430-backup/b1-acceptance && cd ~/msp430-backup/b1-acceptance
SBW=~/Developer/debugprobe-zephyr/tools/msp430/sbw.py
python3 $SBW verify ~/msp430-backup/main-250.hex
python3 $SBW verify ~/msp430-backup/info.hex
```

Expected: `verified` for both (about 25 s for main flash at 250 kHz). `verify` compares exactly the bytes in the files: 0x4400–0x243FF and 0x1800–0x19FF; it does not touch the BSL area. If either reports a difference, **stop** and report the address: the board changed since Phase 6 (possibly the firmware storing data on its own). A new reference read may only be made if the user decides so; it would go beside the Phase 6 backups, never replace them.

- [ ] **Step 3: Acceptance 1: `info` shows debugging**

Run: `python3 $SBW info`
Expected, two lines:

```
MSP430F5529: JTAG ID 0x91, core ID 0x0103, device ID bytes 55h 29h
probe: SBW protocol version 2, debugging supported, 8 hardware breakpoints
```

Anything else in the first line is a hard stop, as in Phase 6.

- [ ] **Step 4: Make the symbol file (outside the repository)**

```bash
cd ~/msp430-backup/b1-acceptance
python3 ~/Developer/debugprobe-zephyr/tools/msp430/symelf.py \
  ~/msp430-backup/fw-rebuild/notes/names.tsv ~/msp430-backup/fw-rebuild/notes/objnames.tsv \
  --image ~/msp430-backup/main-250.hex -o target-symbols.elf
```

Expected: `wrote target-symbols.elf: N functions, M objects, main flash from the image`, N and M the numbers of functions and objects in the local name tables. The `.text` section holds the backup's main flash, so even a `load` that got through every refusal could only carry the backup's own bytes; Step 8 checks that it does not get through.

- [ ] **Step 5: Acceptance 2: attach without reset, registers, detach**

The target is running and its display is on. Terminal 1:

```bash
python3 ~/Developer/debugprobe-zephyr/tools/msp430/sbw.py gdbserver
```

Expected: `waiting for GDB on 127.0.0.1:3333 (in GDB: target remote :3333)`.

Terminal 2:

```
export PATH="$HOME/Developer/msp430-gcc/bin:$PATH"
msp430-elf-gdb -nx ~/msp430-backup/b1-acceptance/target-symbols.elf
(gdb) set remote hardware-breakpoint-limit 8
(gdb) target remote :3333
(gdb) info registers
(gdb) info symbol $pc
(gdb) x/4i $pc
(gdb) detach
(gdb) quit
```

Expected: terminal 1 prints `attached to MSP430F5529 without reset, halted at 0x.....; read-only mode; 8 hardware breakpoints`; `pc` is even and inside main flash (0x4400–0x243FF), `sp` inside RAM (0x1C00–0x43FF); `x/4i` shows plausible MSP430X instructions; terminal 1 prints `detached; the program continues where it stopped`; the user confirms that the display and the keys respond as before. A PC outside main flash, an SP outside RAM, or a target that stops responding: **stop**.

- [ ] **Step 6: Acceptance 3: halt and resume 50 times; the timer while halted**

Stop the server in terminal 1 (Ctrl-C; it prints `interrupted; the GDB server has stopped`). Only one program can use the probe at a time. Then, with the target running:

```bash
cd ~/msp430-backup/b1-acceptance
python3 - <<'EOF'
import os, sys, time
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.expanduser("~/Developer/debugprobe-zephyr/tools/msp430"))
import devices, probe
dev = devices.MSP430F5529
p = probe.SbwProbe(probe.UsbTransport())
p.dap_disconnect(); p.info(); p.configure(250)
a = p.dbg_attach(probe.ATTACH_RUNNING, 250, *dev.ram)
try:
    pcs = [a.pc]
    for _ in range(50):
        p.dbg_resume()
        time.sleep(0.2)
        pcs.append(p.dbg_halt())
    # Timer0_B counter (TB0R, 0x03D0) twice, one second apart, CPU halted.
    # A read of TB0R has no side effect.
    t1 = int.from_bytes(p.read_mem(0x03D0, 1), "little")
    time.sleep(1.0)
    t2 = int.from_bytes(p.read_mem(0x03D0, 1), "little")
finally:
    p.dbg_detach(probe.DETACH_RUN)
print("PCs:", " ".join(f"{x:05X}" for x in pcs))
print("all even and in main flash:", all(x % 2 == 0 and dev.main_start <= x < dev.main_end for x in pcs))
print(f"TB0R while halted: 0x{t1:04X}, one second later 0x{t2:04X}")
EOF
```

Expected: 51 PCs, `all even and in main flash: True`, no exception; the target keeps working (the user confirms). The TB0R line is a measurement, not a pass/fail: equal values mean Timer0_B stops while the CPU is halted, different values mean it keeps counting. Record which.

- [ ] **Step 7: Acceptance 4: hardware breakpoint on a key handler**

`KEY_FUNC` stands for a function from the local symbol table that runs once per key press (chosen in the local acceptance notes), not one that runs on every periodic key poll (that one would stop at once); `KEY_VAR` stands for a variable from the local symbol table (chosen there too). Start the server again in terminal 1 (`python3 .../sbw.py gdbserver`). In terminal 2:

```
(gdb) set remote hardware-breakpoint-limit 8
(gdb) target remote :3333
(gdb) break KEY_FUNC
(gdb) continue
```

The user presses one key that the user named safe. Expected: `Breakpoint 1, 0x000..... in KEY_FUNC ()`, at KEY_FUNC's address or, if the function begins with a prologue GDB recognises (a `PUSHM`, for example), just after it (`break *KEY_FUNC` would stop at the first instruction; see "Using GDB" in `docs/MSP430_DEBUG.md`). Then:

```
(gdb) info registers pc sp
(gdb) x/4i $pc
(gdb) stepi
(gdb) stepi
(gdb) x/1xh &KEY_VAR
(gdb) delete
(gdb) continue
```

Expected: each `stepi` moves the PC to the next instruction shown by `x/4i`; `KEY_VAR` shows a value; after `continue` the target works normally (what the key press started may have lasted longer while the CPU was halted). Then Ctrl-C in GDB (expected: `Program received signal SIGINT`), `detach`, `quit`; the target keeps running. If the breakpoint is not hit after the key press, **stop** and report (do not try other functions).

- [ ] **Step 8: Acceptance 5 and 6: reset and halt; read-only refusals**

In terminal 2 (server still running in terminal 1):

```
(gdb) set remote hardware-breakpoint-limit 8
(gdb) target remote :3333
(gdb) monitor reset
(gdb) flushregs
(gdb) info registers pc
(gdb) x/6i $pc
(gdb) stepi
(gdb) stepi
(gdb) stepi
(gdb) set var *(unsigned short *)0x4400 = 0
(gdb) load
(gdb) break *0x2400
(gdb) continue
(gdb) delete
(gdb) monitor mode full
(gdb) continue
```

Expected, in order: `target reset, halted at 0x.....`, the address in the backup's reset vector (the word at 0xFFFE); `pc` the same address; the start-up code's instructions; three steps; `Cannot access memory at address 0x4400` for the write; `load` fails (GDB's message, and nothing on the probe's side changes); the `continue` after `break *0x2400` fails with `Cannot insert breakpoint 1` (terminal 1: `refused: software breakpoints are not allowed in read-only mode`) and the target stays halted; `full mode is not available in this version (phase B2)`; the last `continue` lets the target start up as after power-on (the user confirms). Then Ctrl-C, `detach`, `quit`, and Ctrl-C in terminal 1.

- [ ] **Step 9: Acceptance 7: flash and information memory equal the backups**

```bash
cd ~/msp430-backup/b1-acceptance
python3 $SBW verify ~/msp430-backup/main-250.hex
python3 $SBW verify ~/msp430-backup/info.hex
```

Expected: `verified` for both. A difference: **stop**; report the address, and do not write anything.

- [ ] **Step 10: Record and commit**

In `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`, Phase 7: replace each `—` in the acceptance table with `✓ <date>` and the measured details (the PC range of Step 6, the TB0R result, the breakpoint hit), or with a short note of what happened; change the `On-target hardware` row to say what was verified. In `docs/MSP430_DEBUG.md`, section "While the CPU is halted", state the TB0R result.

```bash
git add docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md docs/MSP430_DEBUG.md
git commit -m "test: record MSP430 debugging (B1) on-target acceptance results"
```

How the code in this plan was checked while writing it: every code block was built and run in a scratch copy of the repository, task by task in the order of this plan (C++ host tests 193 → 266, under ASan as well; Python tests 148 → 221, the GDB server tests 25 times in a row), the firmware was built for rpi_pico with the feature on and off, and `symelf.py` was run on the user's real name tables (outside the repository). The only expectations written without the real tool are the GDB outputs of Tasks 22, 23 and 25; Task 23 says how to treat a difference.

Type consistency: `Debugger` methods and `Status`/`StopReason`/`AttachMode`/`DetachAction` values are the same in `debug.hpp`, `session.cpp`, the tests and `probe.py` (`ATTACH_*`, `DETACH_*`, `STOP_*`, `STATE_*`, `STATUS_*`); the op numbers match between `protocol.hpp`, `probe.py` and `fake_firmware.py`.

## Spec corrections needed

Places where the spec (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-msp430-debug-design.md`) is wrong or incomplete, and what this plan does instead. The spec itself was not changed.

1. **Target description.** The spec lists `qXfer:features:read+` and a target description "as `msp430-elf-gdb` expects it". GDB's `msp430-tdep.c` never uses target descriptions; the remote register layout is fixed by the gdbarch: 16 raw registers R0–R15, 4 bytes each. The server sends no description. Task 22 checks the layout against the installed GDB.
2. **Architecture selection.** GDB chooses MSP430X and the large code model only from the ELF file's `.MSP430.attributes` section; without it, 16-bit addresses (or "Unknown msp430 isa"). The spec does not mention this; `symelf.py` writes the section.
3. **"A wrong device entry can only make the probe refuse more" did not hold.** With the RAM range taken from the host alone, a wrong (too large) range would let `WRITE_MEM` reach flash. The probe now intersects the host's range with the RAM it knows for the identified device (only the F5529: 0x1C00–0x43FF); for an unknown device a read-only session cannot write RAM at all.
4. **Flash controller registers.** The spec's whitelist allows all of 0x0000–0x0FFF. The plan excludes FCTL1–FCTL4 (0x0140–0x014F), on the probe and on the host.
5. **BSL area in the memory map.** The spec maps main flash, information memory and BSL as `rom`. The plan leaves the BSL area (0x1000–0x17FF) out of the memory map and refuses reads there, because of the Phase 6 observation and the user's rule not to read it on this board.
6. **`READ_MEM`/`WRITE_MEM` while running** return `RUNNING`, not `BAD_STATE`, matching the spec's own definition of `RUNNING`.
7. **Breakpoint count in `info`.** Acceptance step 1 expects `sbw.py info` to show the breakpoint count, but the spec's `INFO` reply has no such field. `INFO` gets a ninth byte, `hw_breakpoints`.
8. **`DBG_STATUS` encoding.** The spec leaves the values open; the plan uses state 0 running / 1 halted, stop reason 0 while running, breakpoint index 0xFF for none, PC 0 while running.
9. **Interrupt stop reply.** After 0x03 the server replies `T02` (SIGINT), not `T05`; breakpoints and steps reply `T05` (`hwbreak:;` for breakpoints).
10. **Name tables.** The user's tables have no `size` column and contain type and member rows (address `-`, kind `struct`, `union`, `member`, with repeating names). `symelf.py` takes the size as optional (0) and skips those rows. With `--image`, only main flash goes into `.text`.
11. **MSPDS device database.** The 3.15.1.1 open-source package does not contain it. The F5529's EEM size (8 triggers), `_1377`/`altRomAddressForCpuRead` and the absence of LPMx.5 settings come from the 3.3.1.3 `TemplateDeviceDb` (BSD-3-Clause).
12. **`PollJStateReg`** is not used for the F5529 (the DLL polls breakpoints with `WaitForEem`); it needs 64-bit shifts and serves LPMx.5 and EnergyTrace. Not ported.
13. **Clock control.** The DLL writes GENCLKCTRL 0x0026 on EEM initialisation, but every CPUXv2 sync macro writes 0x000E (MCLK, SMCLK, ACLK stopped while halted), and the reset-and-halt macro's per-module clock control needs module codes from the DLL's device database. The plan uses 0x000E and leaves module clock control at the device's reset defaults; Task 25 measures Timer0_B while halted.
14. **`FCTL3.BUSY`.** The check can only happen after the sync (the CPU must be under JTAG control to read FCTL3): the port waits up to 100 ms for BUSY to clear before it holds the watchdog, then continues.
15. **Single-step timeout.** MSPDS leaves trigger block 0 set up when the step does not stop in time; the port halts the CPU, restores trigger block 0 and returns `TIMEOUT`.
16. **Smaller points**: `DBG_ATTACH` uses only the normal SBW entry (the magic-pattern entry resets the target); its `flags` bit 0 (full mode) is `BAD_ARG` in B1; `DISCONNECT` in a debug state is `BAD_STATE`, `ERASE` and `FLASH_BEGIN` are `PROTECTED`; GDB's `k` detaches (the program keeps running) instead of killing; `monitor speed` takes effect at the next attach; `--speed` is accepted before and after `gdbserver`; `--allow-flash-write` is refused in B1; the server listens on 127.0.0.1 only.

## Decisions for the user

1. Installing TI's MSP430-GCC (for `msp430-elf-gdb`) into `~/Developer/msp430-gcc` (Task 22, Step 2).
2. Which keys of the board are safe to press during the acceptance (Task 25, Step 7), and confirmation that nothing that could make the firmware store data is done during the session.
3. If the first compare in Task 25 already differs from the Phase 6 backups (for example because the firmware has stored data in flash since then): whether to make a new reference read for this session, kept beside the backups.
4. Whether to fold "Spec corrections needed" 3, 5 and 7 (probe-side RAM table, BSL left out of the memory map, ninth `INFO` byte) back into the spec.

### Answers (2026-11-13)

1. Yes: install TI's MSP430-GCC into `~/Developer/msp430-gcc` in Task 22.
2. The user names the keys that are safe to press when Task 25 reaches that
   step; until then no key is pressed.
3. If the first compare differs: show the difference to the user first; only
   with the user's consent make a new reference read, kept beside the Phase 6
   backups, never replacing them.
4. Done: all corrections are recorded in the spec (3, 4, 5, 7 in its text,
   the rest in its closing section).

### As-built corrections found during execution

- Task 6: the reset-and-halt macro's clock-control value is 0x6D8E
  (`MCLK_SEL3 + SMCLK_SEL3 + ACLK_SEL3 + STOP_MCLK + STOP_SMCLK + STOP_ACLK`
  = 0x6000 + 0x0C00 + 0x0180 + 0x000E, `EEM_defs.h:53-58`,
  `SyncJtag_AssertPor_SaveContextXv2.c:89-91`), not 0x7F8E as written in
  Task 5's `kJclkStopClocks` and Task 6's test vector. Fixed in commit
  `ac6b6a6`.
- Task 6: a failure after the power-on reset of reset-and-halt does a second
  power-on reset (the CPU sits at the safe PC in vacant memory); every
  reset-and-halt failure after the clock switch restores GENCLKCTRL to 0x000E
  first; `wait_for_sync` also checks the JTAG ID. Commits `ac6b6a6`,
  `2255f55`.
- Task 11: the single-step poll ends after 495 reads, as MSPDS's counter
  does (a stop on the 496th read counts as a timeout); trigger block 0 is
  written back on every failure path (EEM off first); a lost JTAG ID after a
  step from low-power mode ends the session. The spec's watchdog sentence on
  single-stepping was corrected (the step restores the firmware's WDTCTL, as
  the DLL does). Commit `52a8d47`.
- Task 13: `DBG_ATTACH` is `BAD_STATE` only in `Connected`/`Flashing`; in a
  debug state it ends the open session with detach-Run and attaches again.
  After the code review the read-only rules were narrowed: `DBG_WRITE_REG`
  to the PC only into main flash or unchanged, and the DMA (0x0500–0x053F)
  and SYS (0x0180–0x019F) registers are not writable. Commits `e194efb`, `028ec17` and the
  follow-up.
- Task 15: after the code review the PMM module (0x0120–0x013F) joined the
  refused ranges on both sides; the GDB map leaves out vacant memory; reads
  of registers with read side effects are refused by default in the GDB
  server (Task 18) with `monitor read` for deliberate reads. For B2: GDB's
  `flash` regions take one block size, so the information memory will need
  to split at 0x1980 (B–D as `flash` with 0x80 blocks; A and the TLV `rom`).
